# PREFACE,
->To the<-
# R E A D E R.

Benevolent Art-loving Reader, just as I have treated various matters in my previous treatises—such as the *Cabinet of Minerals* and the *Golden Lion, or the Vinegar of the Sages*—which have not yet been fully elaborated, and moreover promised several things for which I hear the Seekers <note>original: "Lief-hebbers," literally "lovers [of the art]," referring to dedicated practitioners or amateurs of alchemy.</note> greatly long, so have I wished to share this treatise with them again, which I have named the *Red Lion, or the Salt of the Philosophers*. I had partially touched upon the **Smelting of Minerals** in my **Green Furnace** in my *Cabinet of Minerals*, which is performed without common charcoal, using only dry wood, by the flame of <term>Fire<note>Original: Δ, the alchemical symbol for the element of fire.</note></term>, on a flat hearth, governed upon a common <term>cupel<note>original: "test," a shallow, porous dish used in assaying or refining precious metals to separate them from lead and other base metals through high heat.</note></term>: Now I have [wished to present] this invention to you here completely

<summary>Goossen van Vreeswyk introduces his new treatise, "The Red Lion, or the Salt of the Philosophers," connecting it to his previous works. He highlights his invention of a "Green Furnace" that smelts minerals using wood flames rather than traditional charcoal.</summary>
<keywords>Preface, Goossen van Vreeswyk, Red Lion, Salt of the Philosophers, Green Furnace, smelting, metallurgy, alchemy, wood-fire furnace, cupellation</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical derivation of the "LatentGP" model, specifically focusing on the computational steps of the Expectation-Maximization (EM) algorithm, a strategy for real-time updates, and the setup for experimental validation.</meta>

{y}^j(\theta_{j,t} - y^0_{j,t})} (\frac{\sigma_{j,t}}{\delta_{j,t}})^2\right] \nonumber \\ 
& + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^T \log \sigma_{j,t}^2 + \sum_{t=1}^T \log \delta_{j,t}. \label{eq:variationalObjective_uncond}
\end{align}

Note that we drop the terms $y^j$ which do not contain any trainable parameters. 
Then, we maximize the <term>variational objective<note>A mathematical function used to approximate complex probability distributions by simplifying the problem into an optimization task.</note> from equation (\ref{eq:variationalObjective_uncond}) with respect to the parameters $\phi_j$. 
In summary, we compute the optimized values $\{\phi_j^\star\}_{j=1}^m$ by alternating between two major steps:

1.  **With the model parameters $\{\phi_j\}_{j=1}^m$ fixed, we optimize the hidden states $\{g_t\}_{t=1}^T$:**
    The optimization problem for each sample can be rewritten as:
    \begin{align} \label{eq:L_gt}
    \min_{g_t} L(g_1, \dots, g_T) := \frac{1}{2} \sum_{j=1}^m \sum_{t=1}^T \frac{(y^j_{t} - \mu_{j,t})^2}{\sigma_{j,t}^2} + \frac{1}{2} \sum_{t=1}^T g_t^T \bSigma_t^{-1} g_t
    \end{align}
    Note that the mean $\mu_{j,t}$ and variance $\sigma_{j,t}^2$ depend on the current hidden state $g_t$. This is a classical <term>L-BFGS<note>A popular algorithm in machine learning used for finding the minimum value of a function, particularly efficient for problems with many variables.</note> optimization problem. Since we only have a limited number of hidden variables ($T$) of a relatively small size ($q$), this step is quite efficient.

2.  **With the hidden states $\{g_t\}_{t=1}^T$ fixed, we optimize the model parameters $\{\phi_j\}_{j=1}^m$:**
    Based on our objective function, this is equivalent to:
    \begin{equation} \label{eq:L_phi}
    \min_{\phi_j} L(\phi_j) = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^T \left[ \frac{(y^j_{t} - \mu_{j,t})^2 + \delta_{j,t}^2}{\sigma_{j,t}^2} + \log \sigma_{j,t}^2 + 2\log\delta_{j,t} \right]
    \end{equation}
    The optimal parameter $\phi_j$ can be calculated for each dimension $j$ independently.

Steps 1 and 2 are repeated until the values stop changing significantly (convergence). 
Numerical results in existing studies show that the estimates $\{\phi_j^\star\}_{j=1}^m$ produced by this <term>EM algorithm<note>Short for "Expectation-Maximization," a process that iteratively finds the most likely parameters for a model with hidden variables.</note> are very close to the true parameter values. 
<note>original: "더 구체적인"</note> In Section 1.2, we provide **more specific** hyperparameter optimization details.

## Online Updating Strategy
In the "offline" learning phase (where we process all data at once), the joint distribution of the hidden variables is treated as a product of independent distributions. Here, the hidden states are modeled as <term>Gaussian distributions<note>Also known as a "normal distribution" or "bell curve," used here to represent the probability of a hidden variable's value.</note>. The optimal parameters are found by maximizing the <term>ELBO<note>Evidence Lower Bound: a measure of how well the model's approximation fits the actual data.</note> using the iterative process described above.

In the "online" phase, new data arrives one step at a time (at time $T+1$). To estimate the new hidden variable $g_{T+1}$, we could solve a problem similar to the one in Step 1. However, doing so would require recalculating everything using all past data, which is too slow for large datasets. Instead, we use the results from our offline phase to quickly approximate the prediction for the new time step.

Specifically, we look for a new distribution that maximizes the ELBO for the new data point:
\begin{align} \label{eq:online_elbo}
\mathcal{L}(q(g_{T+1})) = E_{q(g_{T+1})} \left[ \log \frac{p(y_{T+1} | g_{T+1}, \{f_j\}) p(g_{T+1} | \{g_t\}_{t=1}^T)}{q(g_{T+1})} \right]
\end{align}
Under the assumption that the hidden states follow a simple sequence where each step depends only on the previous one (an <term>AR(1) process<note>A "First-order Autoregressive" process, where the current value is a linear function of the immediately preceding value plus some random noise.</note>), this calculation simplifies significantly. The update for the new hidden state becomes:
\begin{align} \label{eq:online_gt}
\hat{g}_{T+1} = \arg \min_{g} \left\{ \frac{1}{2} \sum_{j=1}^m \frac{(y^j_{T+1} - \mu_{j,T+1}(g))^2 + \delta_{j,T+1}^2(g)}{\sigma_{j,T+1}^2(g)} + \frac{1}{2} (g - \mathbf{A}\hat{g}_T)^T \bSigma^{-1} (g - \mathbf{A}\hat{g}_T) \right\}
\end{align}
This online update is very fast because it only requires a small optimization at each new time step.

## Hyperparameter Optimization

The performance of our proposed model, **LatentGP**, is heavily influenced by "hyperparameters"—the settings that define how the <term>Gaussian Process (GP)<note>A powerful tool in statistics used to model smooth, continuous relationships between variables while accounting for uncertainty.</note> behaves. For each GP, these include things like the "signal variance" (how much the data fluctuates), "lengthscales" (how quickly the relationships change over distance), and "noise variance."

In our implementation, we use an iterative EM-like procedure:
*   In the **"E-step,"** we find the best paths for the hidden variables.
*   In the **"M-step,"** we update the GP settings by minimizing the loss function.

Because each dimension's GP is independent, we can optimize them all at once (in parallel). We use an optimizer called L-BFGS-B, which handles constraints well. We also use "automatic differentiation"—a way for the computer to mathematically determine how to adjust the settings to improve the model—making the process robust.

We take special care with the initial settings. For example, we set the noise variance to a very small fraction of the total data variance at the start, allowing the model to focus on capturing the actual signal first. We also update the matrices that govern how the hidden states transition over time to ensure the model remains stable.

# Experiments
In this section, we conduct experiments to see how well **LatentGP** performs. We start with a simple "toy" example to show how it works visually, then test it on two well-known datasets for forecasting to compare it against other standard models.

## Setup
### Baseline Models
We compare **LatentGP** with several representative models:
*   **PCA + AR**: Uses <term>Principal Component Analysis<note>A technique for simplifying complex data by reducing its dimensions while keeping the most important information.</note> for simplification and a standard auto-regressive model for forecasting.
*   **GP**: A standard Gaussian Process applied to each dimension separately.
*   **GP-VAE**: A deep learning model that combines "Variational Autoencoders" with Gaussian Processes [Fortuin et al., 2020].
*   **LSTNet**: A state-of-the-art deep learning model that combines different types of neural networks [Lai et al., 2018].
*   **DeepAR**: A probabilistic forecasting model based on recurrent neural networks [Salinas et al., 2020].

### Evaluation Metrics
To measure performance, we use:
*   **Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE)**: Calculates the average size of the prediction error.
*   **Mean Absolute Percentage Error (MAPE)**: Measures accuracy as a percentage.

Then I have written there also of the Panacea of Antimony <note>original: "Panacea Antimonii"; a universal cure-all made from the mineral antimony, which was highly regarded in 17th-century medical alchemy.</note>; but here I shall teach of the Salt, how one can make the same into a Blood-red Oil, which is the foremost masterpiece of this art.

Furthermore, I have taught there how one can make fine brandies <note>In this context, "brandies" refers to any distilled alcohol or "burning water" (aqua vitae) created from fermented materials.</note> from various kinds of grain, without any foul taste or bad smell; yet here I shall go even further, and teach how to extract good brandies from all kinds of fruits of the field, with various ways to obtain the same without great cost and with great ease. -># PREFACE.<-

I hope that the Brothers in the Art <note>The "Art" refers to alchemy, and the "Brothers" are fellow practitioners and seekers of the Hermetic secrets.</note> will hold me in their best regard, in that I so often write of the Air and the Fire—not indeed the common fire <note>original: "$\triangle$"; the alchemical symbol for the element of Fire.</note>, but the Fire of the earth that causes everything to grow and takes on a body wherever it finds a seed. For this fire <note>original: "$\triangle$"</note> or water <note>original: "$\nabla$"; the alchemical symbol for the element of Water.</note> is the *Sperm* <note>In this context, "sperm" refers to the generative seed or life-force from which all physical matter originates.</note> of all things: such as Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral. However, those who wish to know more of this may read about it in the works of *Sendivogius* <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist famous for his theory that air contains a "hidden food of life."</note>, *Paracelsus*, Count *Bernard* <note>Bernard of Trevisan, a legendary 15th-century Italian alchemist.</note>, and *Basilius* <note>Basilius Valentinus, the supposed Benedictine monk and author of "The Twelve Keys."</note>, where that same thing is depicted with a figure in the Tablet of Hermes <note>The *Tabula Smaragdina* or Emerald Tablet, the foundational text of Hermeticism. The "figure" likely refers to an emblem in a specific edition showing the heavens and earth connected.</note>, namely with hands high in the clouds, to point out to us that the true Matter is nothing other than the Air, which is coagulated by the Sun and the Moon into wood and into stone, even into man and cattle, herbs and flowers. *Paracelsus* says that the Matter is found in Aquarius and Capricorn <note>The zodiac signs for January and February; alchemists often linked specific times of year to the collection or "condensation" of the universal spirit from the air.</note>, of which I shall treat even more broadly hereafter in this Treatise. Yet I wish the favorable friends...

# THE RED LION,

## Or the

### Salt of the Philosophers.

<image-desc>A large decorative drop-cap letter 'D' set within a square woodcut border featuring swirling foliate and floral motifs.</image-desc>

The Art of Alchemy <note>The term "Art" was commonly used by practitioners to describe the skilled work of alchemical transformation.</note> consists in nothing other than in the separation and mixing of substances, which occurs by fire in the melting of Metals and Minerals, as well as in extracting all Salts from them, alongside those from Herbs and Trees—indeed, all fruits of the Earth, which are innumerable. Yet, they are all produced from one and the same nature, just as one can read in the first Chapter of the book of Genesis, where one sees that God first made everything out of water through His Word, when *the Spirit of God moved upon the waters.* <note>Genesis 1:2. Alchemists frequently cited this verse to argue that water was the "prime matter" or fundamental substance of all creation.</note>

If we then wish to take any matters in this Art in hand, it is first and above all necessary for us that we [reduce] the bodies to

The Matter of the Philosophers <note>The *materia prima* or "First Matter," the legendary base substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> is, as far as I can perceive, made of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—yet not the common kind; and these together are bound and locked in a casket, of which God has given the key into the hands of the Artists <note>The "Art" and "Artists" are traditional terms for alchemy and its practitioners.</note> who love Nature and cherish justice.

The true Matter, as far as is known to me, can be nothing other than Earth, Water, and Fire, which together are one, but divided into three, and are brought into the light through the workings of the Art. It is very well known that there is water above the earth, as well as in the earth, and all around it; but it is not seen by us as long as it has not taken on a body. From this Water everything proceeds that is found in the entire world; indeed, it gives us its soul for our sustenance.

This same Water is also called a Magnet, which draws the common water to itself, as can be seen in the book of Job, in the 36th Chapter, at the 27th verse, where he speaks thus: *For he draws up the drops of water, which pour down rain according to its vapor.* <note>Job 36:27. This Matter <note>The *materia prima* or First Matter, the universal base substance.</note> both the poor and the rich have before their eyes; yet the poor use it more commonly, because they must earn their bread more in the sweat of their brow.

Behold the Heavens and the Earth, and you shall see the Matter of the Philosophers, and note the places where it is prepared for us: for it is to be obtained in all places, and it is brought forth there by the power of the Fire, which is hidden in the invisible air, but comes openly through the working of the Sun out of the Earth.

...aware also of what that **Magnesia** might be.

I call that the soul of all things, which gives life to everything, to humans and beasts alike, as well as to all fruits of the land, and also to the Minerals, and whatever else there might be: for God has not only made the Earth as if it were dead, but has placed a spirit or soul into it, which distributes life to everything, of which *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were influential in the 17th century.</note> treats most wonderfully in his 5th Chapter, page 32, with these words: *The life in the earth brings forth all things that grow out of it; therefore, anyone who would then say that the earth were dead would speak no truth at all: for a dead thing cannot give life to anything that would be dead of itself.*

You will perhaps say: I have already read this many times, and have remained just as wise as before. To this I then give you this report: that *Basilius* has revealed more than enough of the **Prima Materia** <term>First Matter <note>The formless, original substance that alchemists believed could be transformed into any other substance.</note></term> in this place; for he calls it the life of the Earth: but it is kept hidden from you by him, wherein this life of the Earth lies enclosed, and where we can obtain it, which the Ancient Sages have still kept concealed. Yet I call that Water and Earth, or Fire and Air: and I do not write this without reason,

# The Red Lion,

# ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

*Plato* <note>In alchemical literature, "Plato" often refers to pseudo-Platonic writings that emphasize the unity of matter and its divine origin.</note> says that our work is made from a single root, and that this root has come forth out of the Earth. Therefore *Arnaldus* <note>Arnaldus de Villa Nova (c. 1240–1311), a celebrated physician and alchemist often cited as a primary authority on the "Stone."</note> writes: *O blessed greenery, and power of the Earth, from which our <term>Red Servant <note>The "Red Servant" or "Red Slave" usually refers to the solar principle or the "Red Man"—an alchemical stage representing the transformation of matter toward the final "Stone."</note></term> is born*; for out of something that is imperfect, one can make a perfect thing: a matter which shall be dealt with further at another time.

The Salt, now, which is hidden in the depths of the Earth, is brought into the open above the Earth, just as we can see in all the fruits of the field. Therefore I say, that the <term>Matter of the Stone <note>The *Materia Prima* or the starting substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note></term> can be nothing else but the fire of the Earth, just as is the Salt; which is why *Christ* spoke in such a manner to his Disciples, saying, *You are the salt of the earth*, and so forth.

The Art of <term>Alchemy</term> is the most despised among all the Arts of the world; and yet it is the basis and the foundation from which all Arts have proceeded. Was Christ Jesus not also the most despised among all men? And yet he has become the salvation of the whole world, indeed the Fountain of life, from which the living water comes that can refresh our souls, which he gives to everyone for free; of which one may read in the *Revelation of John* <note>original: "Openbaring Ioannis." Likely referring to Revelation 21:6: "I will give unto him that is athirst of the fountain of the water of life freely."</note>. One finds also through-

# The Red Lion,

Take 3 or 4 drops in the morning and evening, with <term>water <note>Original symbol: ▽. The downward-pointing triangle is the alchemical symbol for the element of water.</note></term>, wine, or beer. This opens all the internal parts of the body very gently; about which I shall treat more extensively at another time, for it is one of the greatest <term>medicines <note>Original: *Genees-middelen*.</note></term> that I have been able to find in wine or <term>tartar <note>Original: *Wijn-ſteen*. Literally "wine-stone," these are the potassium bitartrate crystals that precipitate out of wine and settle on the sides of barrels.</note></term>. I do not write this from other books, but have found this secret myself through the labor of my own hands. That I have now wished to put it in print is only so that my neighbors might be served by it; for as for myself, I had prepared the same because I wished to seek to make the **Philosopher's Stone** from it; but when I examined everything well, I found myself deceived, because the tartar and the brandy had each already taken their "form above the earth" <note>An alchemical concept suggesting that once a substance has manifested into its final physical state—like a crystal or distilled spirit—it has lost the "primal" or "universal" energy required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. If you now understand this reasoning, then you shall seek that which is not seen, and yet is known by the whole world; of which I shall treat more extensively in the future.

This shall then be distilled in a glass flask in a sand-bath <note>A container of sand used to distribute heat evenly and gently to a glass vessel.</note> with a gentle fire, until the spirit passes over—which comes first—and after that the phlegm <note>Original: *flegma*. Then add your <term>spirit <note>Original Latin: *Spiritus*.</note></term> or essence which you received first, and distill it all once more as before; this must be done four or five times, with dissolving, filtering, and distilling, until you have the true fire out of your dead head.

# The Red Lion

...afflicted by dropsy <note>In historical medicine, dropsy (edema) referred to the painful swelling of the body caused by excess fluid.</note> and gout <note>Original: *Podagra*, a form of inflammatory arthritis that was a common ailment among the wealthy in the early modern period.</note>, as well as for women who suffer from uterine fits <note>Original: *Moer-spel*. This term refers to "the mother" or hysteria, believed at the time to be caused by disturbances of the womb.</note>; giving a little of this, up to 4 or 5 drops with clean rain-water, causes that to be stilled immediately. And those who are swollen from dropsy and gout, when they use five or six drops of this every day, shall be helped of it in a short time. This is also one of the foremost remedies against the bladder stone <note>Original: *steen der Blase*; painful mineral deposits in the urinary tract.</note> in the human body.

<margin>///</margin>
When this spirit has extracted the soul <note>Original Latin: *Anima*. In alchemy, the "soul" is the essential, often colored, essence extracted from a mineral.</note> from the powdered <term>antimony <note>Original symbol: ♁.</note></term>, then it heals the "white flow" or "flowers" of women <note>Historical terms for leucorrhea and menstrual or uterine discharge.</note>. In summary, whoever possesses this spirit can do much more with it than I have described here, especially regarding the metals.

Is the Earth not a coagulated water? Are the fruits of the field not a fire?

# ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

Is the air not a womb of all things, which is invisible to us, and yet from which everything proceeds, through God’s governance, without which nothing can happen?

The <term>Matter of the Stone <note>original: *Materia Lapidis*</note></term> is not a stone, as some dream, but it is a coagulated water and fire. It is white, red, green, yellow, black, blue, and of a thousand different colors; yet I do not possess the <term>Stone <note>original: *Lapidem*</note></term>, but I write only of the Matter which is partially known to me, and which I have revealed to three persons, by the will of God.

The <term>Matter of the Philosophers</term> is a bird that wanders by night, without which no human can live. Its clothes are white during the short days <note>Refers to the winter or perhaps a specific stage in the alchemical process where the "matter" appears white.</note>; and when the dark night comes, it spits out flames of fire; whereby at one time it is loved, and at another time one must be terrified of it. It is flesh, and also has some blood of the earth. It is a <term>Salt</term>, and yet it burns; indeed, it is a red sea of <term>lead <note>Original symbol: ♄ (Saturn). In alchemy, Saturn often represents lead or the "prime matter" at the beginning of the Great Work.</note></term>, which is prepared through common fire. Called by a single word, it is <term>Gold</term>, <term>Salt</term>, <term>Wood</term>, and <term>Earth</term> and nothing else, as far as is known to me; yet not the common <term>Gold</term>, nor <term>Salt</term>, and <term>Wood</term>, but that which the Philosophers call their own, of which <term>Basilius <note>Likely Basilius Valentinus, a celebrated though possibly mythical 15th-century alchemist whose works were highly influential in the 17th century.</note></term> speaks, in his explanation

That one shall take his gold, namely no common gold, but that which has been brought forth by Nature. This same gold is also called a <term>mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note></term> and a <term>sulphur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note></term>, p. 165.

The Matter of the Philosophers I call a sulphur of the earth, which at its proper time can be obtained in all places; namely, when the days are shortest and the nights are longest <note>Refers to the Winter Solstice, a period many alchemists believed was best for collecting "celestial" or "universal" salts from the air.</note>; where one has the highest mountains and the deepest valleys, there is the <term>Sal Armoniac <note>Ammonium chloride; in alchemy, it represents a volatile spirit that can bridge the gap between solid and vapor.</note></term> of the Wise, the Saltpeter of <term>Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist known for his theory that the "food of life" is hidden in the air.</note></term>, the Red Servant of <term>Bernard <note>Bernard of Trevisan, a legendary 15th-century alchemist.</note></term>, the river that flows around the land of the Moors <note>An allegorical reference to a source of primal matter, often linked to the biblical Gihon or the Nile.</note>, where one obtains the Gold of the Wise, which has come forth in the Garden of <term>Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical father of the Hermetic arts.</note></term>, and is distilled out of the highest mountains through the power of the Sun and the Moon into the Philosophical Sea, or the Red Sea of the Wise. # The Red Lion,

This <term>Matter</term> <note>The "Prima Materia" or first material required for the alchemical "Great Work."</note> is nothing other than a water and air, fire and earth, ashes and salt, dew and rain, hail and snow, wind, <term>Vitriol</term> <note>A historical term for various sulfate minerals, such as iron or copper sulfate, used for their acidic properties.</note>, <term>Saltpeter</term>, <term>Alum</term>, <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride; a salt used in alchemy for its ability to sublimate and interact with metals.</note>, along with many more other names, under which the Sages have hidden it, so that out of a thousand, scarcely one can attain it. ### or the Salt of the Philosophers.

...and sour of taste, and possesses within itself all things that are found in the entire world. This <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: Δ</note> or <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ∇</note> is distilled through its own fine <term>Material</term> <note>The "Prima Materia" or first matter.</note>, which was previously its fine <term>Mother</term> <note>In alchemy, the "Mother" refers to the base substance or matrix that "gives birth" to the transformed chemical.</note>, yet I write this only by way of parable, because the Philosophers agree on the making of this water, as well as from what Material it can be drawn; and even if I called it by its right name, no one would believe it. # ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

Wine-vinegar. When one then desires this as a ferment, it must be done in the following way.

Then, one must let this dissolve into a <term>Water</term> <note>Latin: *Aqua*; here referring to the liquid formed when salt absorbs moisture from the air</note> in the open air, and filter <note>original: "diftilleren," though in this context it refers to straining through a filter</note> it through felt-paper until the Water has become clear; then it shall be ready for the work.

Afterward, take this Water of <term>Salt</term> <note>🜔</note> and the Water of <term>Antimony</term> <note>♁</note> in <term>equal parts</term> <note>Latin: *ana*</note>; pour these together in small portions; for when these two come together, they make a great racket and overflow the glass, and therefore you must be careful in the pouring; for it produces a great ferment <note>a violent chemical reaction</note> before these two are willing to "die" <note>alchemical terminology for the reaction reaching completion and settling</note>. And when these have finished reacting, <margin>/</margin>one extracts the <term>Alkahest</term> <note>Often referred to as a universal solvent, here meaning a potent medicinal essence</note> from the <term>Salt</term> <note>🜔</note> <margin>/</margin>with the distilled <term>Water</term> <note>🜄</note> of the <term>Antimony</term> <note>♁</note>, so that you then <margin>/</margin>receive a beautiful powder, which is one of <margin>/</margin>the primary remedies against the <term>Spanish Pox</term> <note>A historical name for Syphilis</note> and all the ailments that <margin>/</margin>attend it. ## Commission for Mr. Vreeswijk.

The West India Company <note>Referring here to the French West India Company (*Compagnie des Indes occidentales*), established in 1664 to manage French colonies in the Americas</note>, to Mr. Goossen van Vreeswijk, Greetings. Just as the Land of <term>Canada</term> or <term>New France</term> is found to be abundant in various kinds of <term>Mines</term>, of which the assays <note>tests performed on ore to determine its metal content</note> that have been made give hope that one could derive significant profit from them, if the management thereof is entrusted to a person understanding such matters and well-experienced in these activities; So have we, Directors General of the said Company, being fully informed of your capability and experience in the knowledge of <term>Mines</term>, and that you could extract from them the utility they can provide with as little expense as possible; Further, relying on your faithfulness and good inclination, we have by these presents ordered and authorized you, even as we do order and authorize you, to have the supreme management over the working of all the <term>Mines</term> in the aforementioned Land of <term>Canada</term> or <term>New France</term>, the opening of the

# Concerning Mining Matters, and the Office of a Mining Master in General.

## Article 1.

A Mining Master <note>original: "Berg-meeſter," a high-ranking official who oversaw both the technical and administrative operations of a mine</note> must reside near the Mine, in order to keep a daily eye on the labor of the common folk <note>original: "gemeene volk," referring to the laborers or miners</note>, and to keep a precise daily record of that which is obtained and extracted from the Mine. The Foreman <note>original: "Meeſter-knecht," literally "master-servant," the head workman in charge of the shifts</note> shall show these same yields to the Mining Master, so that the Mining Master can perform an assay <note>original: "proeve," a chemical test to determine the purity or value of the ore</note> of the Mineral, to know what is coming out of the Mine daily.

## Article 2.

A Mining Master must take good heed of the site, and of the nature of the stone that is extracted and hewn loose there every day, by which one can see the life or the death of the Mine, just as I have described in my *Cabinet of Minerals* <note>Goossen van Vreeswijk published several works on mineralogy and alchemy, including "Het Cabinet der Mineralen"</note>. One can also perceive this by the color of the stone; for if one sees white, blue, or lime-like spots on the stones, it is a sign that the Mine is in decline and lives no further, which I have found to be true.

<sig>C 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: A</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the primary duties of a Mining Master, emphasizing the need for constant supervision, daily record-keeping of ore extraction, and the ability to judge the "life" or exhaustion of a mine based on the geological characteristics of the stone.</summary>
<keywords>Mining Master, Berg-meester, mineralogy, assaying, mine management, Cabinet of Minerals, Goossen van Vreeswijk, ore quality</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the Mining Master’s duties, transitioning from the observation of geological signs to the management of subterranean water and the health hazards of mine vapors.</meta>

->The Red Lion<-

### Article 3.

A Mining Master must pay close attention to whether any water-spring <note>original: "water-fontein," referring to groundwater breaking into a mine shaft</note> might reveal itself during the work, and then he shall not allow that place to be worked any longer, unless it is at its end; but if he can lead that water away by hand <note>meaning to divert the flow using drainage channels or pipes</note>, then he is free to proceed further therein. Therefore, a Mining Master must have good knowledge of the waters under the earth, whether they run against the Sun or with the Sun <note>Early modern miners believed the direction of subterranean water relative to the sun’s path—East to West—influenced the "ripening" and "origin" of metallic ores</note>; for by this one can know what sort of origin that Mine might have; of which more shall be spoken in another place. This much is known: that the waters running against the Sun are subject to many cold and poisonous vapors, which cause many illnesses among the folk, such as swollen legs <note>likely edema or dropsy caused by the harsh, damp conditions</note> and the swelling of bellies, from which many must die. And if this is noticed, the Mining Master must apply his diligence to overcome these vapors by means of bellows or smoke-work <note>original: "geblaes of rook-werk"; techniques for mine ventilation, using bellows to pump air or smoke to identify air currents and clear "damps" or toxic gases</note>, and then rid himself of the water.

A Mining Master is required, after all...

mining laws <note>original: "Berg-rechten," the legal framework governing mining rights and claims</note>, to have those places worked where any minerals are seen or perceived: and he must take good note whether there is a flowing river at that place, in which the minerals can be washed of their earthiness <note>referring to the "washing" process where raw ore is cleaned of soil and debris</note> that remains clinging to them from the mountain-ore; as well as whether that same water is suitable to set a mill there, where the minerals may be smelted <note>waterwheels were used to power bellows for furnaces and heavy stamps for crushing rock</note>.

### Article 5.

A Mining Master <term>Berg-meester: the head official of a mining jurisdiction, responsible for both technical oversight and the administration of mining law</term> must also observe whether there might be a good forest near that place, in order to obtain wood in abundance, both for the construction of the Mine <note>timber was essential for shoring up tunnels and shafts</note>, as well as for burning the charcoal <note>original: "kolen"; at this time, "coal" almost always referred to charcoal produced from wood, which was the primary fuel for smelting</note> by which the mineral is smelted. And in case one should lack such things, then it is not advisable to begin that work, unless one had a convenient passage <note>a road, path, or waterway</note> to be able to fetch and bring them to the place where the smelting furnace is.

## Article 3.

A Mining Master <term>Berg-meester: the head official responsible for the technical, legal, and administrative oversight of a mining district</term> must take good heed whether any water-spring <note>original: "water-fontein," referring to an underground source or sudden influx of water</note> might reveal itself during the work, and if so, he shall not allow that place to be worked any further, as it is then at its end; but if he can divert that water, he is free to continue there. Therefore, a Mining Master must have good knowledge of the waters under the earth, and whether they run against the sun or with the sun <note>In early mining lore, the direction of water flow or ore veins relative to the sun’s path (East to West) was thought to indicate the quality and "ripeness" of the minerals</note>; for by this one can know what kind of origin that Mine may have; of which more shall be spoken in another place. This much is certain: that the waters which run against the sun are subject to many cold and poisonous vapors <note>likely referring to "damp," the dangerous gases like methane or carbon dioxide that accumulate in mines</note>, which cause many illnesses among the people, such as swollen legs and the swelling of bellies <note>edema or dropsy, often caused by poor nutrition, damp conditions, or mineral poisoning</note>, from which many must die. And if this is noticed, the Mining Master must apply his diligence to overcome these vapors through blowing <note>using bellows to force fresh air into the shafts</note> or smoke-work <note>a ventilation technique using fires to create a draft that draws out stale air</note>, and then rid himself of the water.

## Article 5.

Since mineral coal was not yet widely used for smelting, forests were vital for producing the charcoal needed to reach high temperatures</note>, through which the Mineral is smelted. And if one should have a lack of such things, then it is not advisable to begin that work, unless one had a convenient passage to be able to fetch and bring them to the place where the smelting furnace is.

## Article 7.

A Mining Master <term>Bergmeester: the head official of a mining jurisdiction</term> must divide all the places of the Mountains where any Minerals are perceived into suitable portions; such as an Iron-Mine into two or four parts; yet especially according to the suitability of the places and the direction of the Veins. ### Article 10.

A Mine Master must every day make a complete assay <term>preuve</term> <note>A chemical test to determine the purity and content of metal within an ore sample</note> of the mineral <term>Minerael</term> that comes out of the mine, so that one can know what kind of metal <term>Metael</term> is delivered daily by that mine. Furthermore, a Mine Master shall make assays of all the marcasites <term>Marcasiten</term> <note>A historical term for various metallic sulfide minerals, often believed to be the "seed" or "mother" of metals</note>, whether they be salts, saltpeter <term>Salpeter</term>, vitriol <term>Vitriool</term> <note>Sulfate minerals, essential for various early industrial and alchemical processes</note>, alum <term>Aluin</term>, or salt; or of all minerals of a sulfurous <term>Solferachtigen</term> nature. These assays the Mine Master shall make known to his Lord in secret.

### Article 11.

A Mine Master must, with his mining compass <term>Berg-kompas</term>, reveal, divide, and bring into order the veins <term>Gangen</term> <note>The mineral-rich paths or passages through the rock</note>, so that no misunderstanding occurs in the working; for to work in the mine without a compass is vanity. Therefore, the mining compass shall have a sharp needle, and not be like the common ship's compass—

### Article 13.

A Mine Master <term>Berg-meester</term> must not allow a place to be hewn out that is by nature too hard, but should avoid it, and even go around it a bit, until one comes into the mountain [proper] and can use fire <note>This refers to "fire-setting," an ancient mining technique where rock faces were heated with fire and sometimes quenched with water to make the stone crack and crumble</note>, to thus break the stones into pieces, whereby the mineral is then exposed. This has also happened to me, that I had to set to work with fire; but when one begins to stir these stones, much stench of Sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> reveals itself, which the people cannot well endure; for all the Sulfur that comes from there is mixed with a Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and *Spirit* of Lead <note>original: "Spiritus ♄"; in alchemy, Saturn (♄) represents the metal Lead</note>, which then enters the bodies of the people, and they do not easily get rid of it again: these then loosen their gums, so that almost all the teeth fall out of the mouth <note>The author is describing the symptoms of heavy metal poisoning, likely from mercury or lead vapors released during the heating of the ore</note>. Concerning these illnesses, *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note> writes extensively enough in his book *On Mountain Sicknesses* <note>original: "Berg-siekten"; Paracelsus wrote "Von der Bergsucht und anderen Bergkrankheiten," one of the first works on occupational medicine</note>, in the 4th part.

Thus I now wish to warn all Mine Masters and miners who shall practice in such work, to take a supply of a good medicine, in order to keep all the people in health, so that the labor may proceed in the working of the mine. In this I can recommend to you no better [remedy] than that which I myself...

In order to not only present this furnace <note>term: Furnace (Oven) — The central heat source for alchemical operations, which must be carefully constructed to maintain specific temperatures for smelting and calcination.</note> to the eye, I shall also provide the instructions for its construction and assembly, as well as its use, here; it may be built larger or smaller according to the requirements of one's work.

For this, you must use good stones that can withstand the fire and never melt; otherwise, all your labor would be in vain. For if the stone of the furnace were to melt into the mineral, it would be with great difficulty that you would be able to remove that obstruction again.

### *The size and proportion of this furnace*
### *is as follows.*

Four feet high from the ground, and five and a half feet long, four and a half feet wide; the vault <note>original: "verhemelsel" — here referring to the arched interior ceiling of the furnace</note> above should be rounded like a baking oven and three and a half feet high. The mouth through which the coals are introduced, as well as the minerals or metals for smelting, should be two feet; the ash-hole must be one foot square; the hole through which the wood is stoked and the fire maintained shall be half a foot square. The grate must be two feet from the ground. At the top of the furnace, there must be four smoke-holes, each a hand-breadth wide, so that one can plug them with a stone

This furnace is one of the greatest secrets in the art of smelting: and first, nothing else is used here for the fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> but simple dry wood; whereas in other smelting furnaces one must have charcoal, which proves to be considerably more expensive. ### This Green Furnace is now used in this manner,

When one wishes to smelt in this furnace, one must first let it become thoroughly dry in every respect, and that of its own accord <note>original: "van ſich ſelven" — meaning air-drying naturally without the application of heat.</note>, before one shall bring any <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: Δ</note> into it: for if it is not properly dried before any <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: Δ</note> enters it, it would fall apart, and you would suffer loss <note>Likely referring to the loss of both the materials and the labor invested in the furnace.</note>. When it has thus become dry, then bring your fire into the furnace; and it having become perfectly hot, you may put the metal in there that you wish to smelt; and then give it good <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: Δ</note>, until everything is molten: then pull the iron plug out from it, so you can pour from the metal whatever you desire: and when that metal is out, you can once again bring other material into it as before, and smelt as long as it shall please you. And while this furnace is so hot or warm, it can be maintained with a small fire, and the metal then melts sooner than before.

# *On the parting of Gold and Silver.*

Some people wish to judge that the gold would become fine through <term>Royal Water</term> <note>original: "Aqua Regis" — a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids, so named because it can dissolve "royal" metals like gold.</note>; but that is false: for I shall prove with firm reasons that no true separation can occur through the "waters" <note>Alchemists used the term "waters" to refer to various corrosive acids.</note>, such as [separating] the mercury from <term>silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, or the mercury from gold; since these waters dissolve the mercury just as well as silver and gold. For example, take silver and dissolve it with <term>Strong Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜅; also known as "Aqua Fortis" or nitric acid.</note>; then add a little common rain-water

whereby, in order to weaken the <term>acid</term> <note>original symbol: ▽. While the triangle usually denotes Water, in this context it refers to *Aqua Fortis* (nitric acid).</note>; then lay some <term>copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀ (Venus)</note> in that *water* <note>meaning the acid</note> in which the silver is; you shall not have weakened that same water so much that the copper will not also dissolve in it: therefore this dissolving or parting is false; and even more so when the acid is made from <term>saltpeter</term> <note>original symbol: ☽ (the Moon), often used in this context for saltpeter</note> and <term>vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: ⊕ (Earth), commonly used for vitriol or salts</note>, for it carries over a volatile copper, which then takes on a body that will be very difficult to separate from the pure metals without a reduction of the *Body* <note>the physical mass of the metal</note>; for such spirits do not separate from silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note> or gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> without taking a spoil from them. Therefore I shall advise no one to separate their metals through the aforementioned acid or *Aqua Regis* <note>original: "Aqua Regis"—Royal Water, a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids</note>: for just as one never enters brothels without having to leave behind one's fine feathers <note>a Dutch idiom meaning to lose one's wealth or reputation</note>, so it is also with silver and gold, which do not come unscathed from <term>Venus</term> <note>In alchemy, Venus represents Copper. The author is making a pun on the "greeting of Venus" representing both the chemical reaction with copper and the dangers of a brothel.</note>, where the greeting of Venus consumes the strength of the metals. And if some should imagine that such metal was still as good as that which first comes out of the earth, they are mistaken. I speak of the gold or the silver that comes in its fine nature out of the earth, and has not yet been in the <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, nor has been mixed with any *Body*: yet he who has not had this in his own hands will not easily be able to believe it.

->### or the Salt of the Philosophers.<-

One can separate <term>copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀ (Venus)</note> from <term>silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☽ (the Moon)</note> by using *Aqua Regis* <note>original: "Royal Water," a powerful mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids</note>: for that attracts the copper and leaves the silver behind. This is quite effective if the silver has been separated from the <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉ (the Sun)</note> in the common manner by an <term>acid</term> <note>original symbol: ▽ (Water). In this context, it refers to the liquid solvent or "strong water" used for the parting of metals.</note>; for then the copper and silver are contained together in one liquid. This same water is then drawn off, and your silver—which you obtained from or out of the gold—is melted using a fluxing powder; just as I taught in *The Golden Lion*, as well as in *The Cabinet*, where you may consult that information.

Now we shall return again to the "Red Servant" of *Bernardus* <note>likely Bernard of Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist</note>, wherein lies the fountain of this art. *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, the legendary author of various alchemical treatises</note> calls this a <term>sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍. In alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible, and "soul" principle of a substance.</note>, and he adds to it a "Red Lion" on page 68, saying: *“When this—namely the sulfur—has been extracted from the Red Servant, it shall be dissolved in its own blood, from which it originally emerged.”*

On page 24, in his second chapter, he calls it an "Eagle" <note>often a symbol for sal ammoniac or the volatile spirit of mercury</note> and a "Cold Dragon" <note>often a symbol for saltpeter or the fixed crystalline state of a mineral</note>, and teaches there how one should handle the same with the following words: *“If you take the Eagle with the Cold Dragon—which has long made its dwelling in the rocky cliffs and caverns of the earth, slithering in and out—and place these together in Hell <note>the bottom or hottest part of the alchemical furnace</note>, then Pluto <note>the Roman god of the underworld, here representing the heat of the furnace</note> shall blow upon them, and that shall [overcome] the cold”*

# The Red Lion,

Dragon drives out a volatile spirit, which through its great heat shall burn the Eagle’s feathers <note>In alchemy, the "Eagle" often represents Mercury or a volatile substance; "burning the feathers" refers to fixing a volatile substance so it no longer evaporates.</note>, whereby a sweat-bath is prepared that causes the snow on the peaks of the mountains to melt and become water, so that the mineral bath may be prepared, and the King may have happiness and health. Nature works in it alone:

Until the Soul is given back to him again.

# or the <term>Salt of the Philosophers</term>. <note>In alchemical theory, Salt is the third principle alongside Sulfur and Mercury, representing the body, stability, and the "fixed" physical substance remaining after distillation.</note>

Red is the Father, who can exist through the spirit; and the white is <term>Earth</term> <note>Original symbol: ▽. The downward-pointing triangle is the standard alchemical symbol for the element of Earth.</note>. These come forth from one and the same thing. The Red is volatile, about which <term>Flamel</term> <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), a French scrivener who posthumously gained a reputation as an alchemist who discovered the Philosopher’s Stone.</note> has written in his Book. The fixed substance is the whiteness of the earth, of which <term>Sendivogius</term> <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist and philosopher famous for his "New Chemical Light."</note> writes on page 63: That it is just like a <term>calcined Tartar</term> <note>Potassium carbonate produced by heating the crusty deposits from wine barrels; used in alchemy as a powerful flux or cleansing agent.</note>. <term>Basilius</term> <note>Basil Valentine, the supposed 15th-century Benedictine monk whose writings are a cornerstone of late medieval alchemy.</note> proceeds further to the joining together, as follows.

> *Those two and three are still but one,*
> *If you understand it not, you shall achieve none.*
> *Does Adam not sit in the water-bath,*
> *In which Venus had her equal?*
> *This the old Dragon has prepared for her,*
> *Who laments his lost strength.*
> *It is no more, says the Philosopher,*
> *Than doubled <term>Mercury</term>.* <note>Original symbol: ☿. "Doubled Mercury" often refers to a substance that has been purified and combined with its own internal spirit, or "Mercury of the Wise."</note>

Basilius acts here like a Father, when he so favorably names the <term>Matter</term> for us, saying that it is made from a single thing. He calls the <term>Mercury</term> [☿] "Adam," showing us that one shall place him in the <term>water-bath</term> <note>The *balneum Mariae*, or bain-marie; a method of gentle heating by placing a vessel in hot water to avoid direct flame.</note>, which he calls a "Red Sea" elsewhere. # The Red Lion,

Basil <note>Basilius Valentinus (Basil Valentine), a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings are foundational to the Hermetic tradition.</note> says that his Gold had a Magnet, which is the first root of the <term>Great Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the "Great Work," believed to transmute metals and grant long life.</note>; this one reads in his Concluding Speech. To come now to the conclusion of Basil's Rhyme, he speaks thus:

> *I say no more, it has been named;*
> *He who knows it well may be acclaimed.*
> *Seek that, and never grow tired or weary,*
> *For the outcome proves the deeds.* <note>original: "Exitus acta probat." A Latin legal and philosophical maxim suggesting that the result is the ultimate test of an action.</note>

Basil presents in this verse everything there is to know, both regarding the Matter and the work. And on page 138, in his *Microcosm* <note>The concept of the "Microcosm" views the human being as a small-scale reflection of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note> he further writes: *That all the world has this Matter before its eyes, and that it is manifest; namely <term>Mercury</term> <note>Original symbol: ☿.</note>, <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Original symbol: 🜍.</note>, and Salt, a mineral <term>Fire</term> <note>Original symbol: △.</note>, or a mineral Liquor, as if separated from the Center of its Form, and made from these three principles.*

Water and fire are against one another, yet water is brought forth from the fire; and thereafter the fire is daily fed and maintained by the water. This one can see when a fire in an <term>Oven</term> <note>In alchemy, the oven or furnace (athanor) was designed to maintain a steady, "natural" heat for months at a time.</note> has been lit by a portion of wood; if one did not come to feed that fire again with wood, then it would go out, and it would then have to die. Now wood and all <term>Vegetables</term> <note>In this period, "vegetables" referred generally to all plant life or the "vegetative soul" of growing things.</note>

I then took 40 pounds of common earth, which seemed to me to be fertile; then I took a tub, put that earth into it, and set it in a suitable place, so that no other earth came near it. In this earth I planted the seed of a <term>watermelon</term> <note>Original: "water-limoen," literally "water-lime."</note>, or <term>Spanish bacon</term> <note>Original: "Spaens spek," a historical Dutch colloquialism for a variety of melon, likely named for its thick rind or sweet flesh.</note>, and let it stand there for three or four months, or as long as it took for that watermelon to become fully grown. This was in the <term>West Indies</term> <note>A reference to the Caribbean or the Americas, indicating the author’s travels and the tropical setting of the experiment.</note>, where I had much to do with other matters, so that at the time I did not reflect upon it as deeply as I did later when [I...]

<page-num>65</page-num>
<header>or the Salt of the Philosophers.</header>

...I have well experienced; namely, that everything comes forth from the power of the Δ <term>Fire</term> <note>The triangle symbol Δ represents the element of Fire.</note>, which is hidden in the water: and this same fire is the matter of the **Stone of the Wise**, as I believe.

I have called this fire in the **Golden Lion** ⊕ <term>Antimony</term> <note>The symbol ⊕ refers here to Antimony, often called the "Grey Wolf" or "Golden Lion" in alchemy when purified.</note>, or **Vinegar of the Wise**, which shall be drawn from the **Rays of the Sun**, and brought forth by the **Moon**.

All **Minerals**, and whatever adheres to them, first have their origin in water, and afterward, once the **Sun** has worked within it, the water receives a **Body**, according to the seed from which it is received; and thus the **Matter** then comes to light, as in Flowers, Sprouts, Wood, Stone, Salts, Minerals, Metals—yes, even that which was hidden in the water itself. I say then further, that no man shall come who will rightly describe the power of water and of fire; for that is the true **Matter** of all things, and in that very same is hidden the **Stone of the Philosophers**, of which the **Ancient Wise** have spoken so much. I say then, everything that gives off a smoke is ☿ <term>Mercury</term>, and that which burns is a 🜍 <term>Sulfur</term>, and that which still remains I call a **Salt**. But he who cannot believe this may do as I have done, and perhaps he will then find what he seeks.

<sig>E</sig>
<meta>catchword: Of</meta>

<summary>The author concludes that all physical matter originates from a "hidden fire" within water, using his watermelon experiment as proof. He defines the three alchemical principles—Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt—based on how a substance reacts to fire (smoking, burning, or remaining as ash).</summary>

### A powder for Sweating, or Diaphoretic Antimony. <insert>Kenelm Digby <note>Sir Kenelm Digby (1603–1665) was a renowned English courtier, diplomat, and natural philosopher known for his medical recipes.</note></insert>

<margin>Quincy's Dispensatory <note>A reference to John Quincy’s *Pharmacopoeia Officinalis & Extemporanea*, a highly influential pharmaceutical text of the 18th century.</note></margin>

From Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> a Medicine is made to induce sweating.

**Recipe:** One pound of **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, and one pound of **Nitre** <note>original symbol: 🜔; also known as saltpeter, used here as an oxidizing agent</note>: pound these well together into a powder. Then set the same on fire with a piece of glowing charcoal, and cover it somewhat with a board, yet not so tightly that it would smother. When that has been done, then take a melting pot or crucible, and place your Matter therein, which you shall calcine <note>heat to a high temperature to remove volatile substances</note>, yet not with such a great **Fire** <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> that it would melt, but only so that it becomes red: this must stand there no longer than a quarter of an hour; then you shall take it out, dissolve it in rain **water** <note>original symbol: ♒︎</note>, and filter <note>original: "diftilleren"; in this context, it refers to straining or filtering through paper</note> it through a felt-paper, so the powder remains in the paper. This you shall then dry, and add as much fresh **Nitre** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> thereto; which you must then treat again as before, by burning it, calcining it in the **Fire** <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>, dissolving it in the **Water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>, and filtering it: after that, burn it once more for the third time with **Nitre** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, and then edulcorate <note>original: "afgefoet"; the process of washing a precipitate to remove soluble salts</note> it with rain **water** <note>original symbol: ♒︎</note> and dry it; then your Medicine is ready to be used. Its Dose is from 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, up to 10 grains.

# The Red Lion,

Others have also written about this, but the preparation is distinct: for some take for that work not only **Nitre** <note>original symbol: ⊖; likely referring to saltpeter or a similar corrosive salt flux</note>, but also **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>; yet that is not as good, because the **Nitre** partly kills itself, biting and eating upon the **Antimony**, where it then almost leaves the **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> behind, so that the **Mercury** is not sufficiently **fixed** <note><term>fixed</term>: in alchemy, to "fix" a substance means to transform it from a volatile or liquid state into a solid form that can endure fire without evaporating.</note>. Also, this Medicine is then not as white or bright as through the **Nitre** alone. And as proof that this preparation, where the **Antimony** is included, is not as well fixed, read about it in the *Golden Lion*, where I teach how to make a Medicine from **Mercury** using **Nitre** and **Antimony** *in equal parts* <note>original: "ana."; a pharmaceutical abbreviation from the Greek 'ana', meaning equal amounts of each ingredient</note> to serve as a purge; yet after it is used.

<margin>/ Green Lion 139. <note>A reference to another alchemical text or perhaps a specific chapter/page in the author's broader work.</note></margin>

Some also call this a *Saffron of Metals* <note>original: "Crocus Metallorum"; a reddish-brown powder usually made by calcining antimony with nitre</note>, but entirely wrongly: for how can a **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> or Mineral provide a *Saffron*, since it is not a Metal? However, I still intend to treat this in another Treatise, where I shall write of the death of Metals: for a *Saffron* is not a **calx** <note>original: "kalk"; the crumbly powder remaining after a metal has been thoroughly "burnt" or oxidized in a furnace</note>. I mean to say that there is a difference between dust and ashes: of which **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 17th century.</note> also speaks, as I have related before. I say then that the ashes give a **Salt**, and the dust gives a **Mercury**. Do you understand this well? Out of the water comes forth the fire; this same changes again into ashes, and at last it becomes dust. The wind

# The Red Lion,

If you do not know this nature, you will not know what a tree or a herb consists of, since they all come forth from one ground, from one power, and from one Master: for without **water** <term>▽</term> <note>original symbol: ▽; representing the element of Water, considered by alchemists to be the primary building block of all physical things.</note> God has made nothing in the world; just as we read in the book of Genesis. <note>A reference to the biblical creation story where the Spirit of God moved over the "face of the waters" (Genesis 1:2).</note> Therefore, you must highly esteem the **water** <term>▽</term> of Heaven, which causes everything to come forth.

   Therefore I tell you in truth, so far as experience in the investigation of **Matters** <note>In alchemy, 'Matter' refers to the raw substances—mineral, vegetable, or animal—selected for transformation in the laboratory.</note> has made known to me, that this nature is nothing other than the **Mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; the metal Mercury, but here specifically 'Philosophical Mercury,' the volatile, life-giving spirit hidden within matter.</note> of the earth, * which is drawn upward by the Sun, which I have partly seen in the form of a beautiful **water** <term>▽</term>, like a teardrop <note>original: "oogen-traen"; this likely refers to the "tears" or beads of condensation seen on the inside of a glass vessel during distillation.</note>, distilled out of its own earth; it was first brought forth from the **water** <term>▽</term>, and the **water** <term>▽</term> is held within it by the power of the earth, for it is a distilled earth, air, and fire, wrought by the Sun and the Moon: yet from this **Matter** and first origin comes much good and evil, which shall be dealt with further in another place.

### The Distilling of Brandies.

In *The Golden Lion* <note>Likely a reference to a previous book or treatise written by the author.</note> I have written about the distilling of **Grain-Brandies** <term>Brandewynen</term> <note>Literally "burnt wine," the origin of the word brandy; here referring to spirits distilled from fermented grain.</note>, and earlier in this volume I have touched upon it somewhat; yet I feel compelled to speak even more of it now, because it grieves me and pains my heart to see that the beautiful, dear **Bread-grain** <note>original: "Brood-koorn"; grain specifically reserved for making bread to feed the population.</note>, such as Wheat and Rye, is so excessively destroyed in the making of such drink; and it is much to be feared that we shall thereby provoke God to anger, and bring times of famine <note>original: "diere tyden"; literally "dear times," referring to periods of high prices and food scarcity.</note> upon our own necks. *Why do you spend money for that which is not bread? and your labor for that which does not* <note>A partial quote from the biblical Book of Isaiah 55:2, questioning why people waste resources on things that do not nourish them.</note>

If someone were now inclined toward this <note>Referring to the cultivation of wine mentioned on the previous page.</note>, they shall ensure that their lands are first suitably prepared as they ought to be, because young **vineyards** <term>Wijngaerden</term> require a loose, rich soil.

# The Red Lion, 86

<margin>/</margin> ...[In] France, in the province of **Normandy** <note>original: "Normandien"</note>, where they make a drink from their apples similar to wine, which they call <term>Cider</term> or **Apple-drink** <term>Appel-drank</term>. This drink possesses a very great power; for when a person has a jug of it in their body, they become as joyful as if they had drunk the best wine. From this **Apple-drink**, the best **Brandy** <term>Brandewijn</term> <note>literally "burnt wine," the historical term for distilled spirits</note> can be made that one could ever wish for; one must add a <term>ferment</term> of **sourdough** <term>suurdeeg</term> to it, so that it begins to work <note>ferment</note>; and once it is distilled, it readily releases its spirit, which is the **Brandy**.

<margin>/</margin> If you also put sour apples into a vat or barrel, so that three parts remain empty and only one part is filled with apples (which must first be broken or crushed into pieces), then fill the rest of the barrel with clean, sweet rainwater or river water. Place your barrel in a warm room <note>The Dutch "strove" refers to a heated chamber or drying room</note> or a place where there is a **fire-stove** <term>vyer-kachel</term>, and your mixed Matter will begin to work, from which you can then make good **Brandy**. And if you wish to obtain a **Vinegar** <term>Asijn</term> from the same, you must let it stand for 10 or 12 weeks, by which time it will have all turned into **Vinegar**, which you can then draw off. # The Red Lion

<margin>]</margin>From the common peas that we eat daily, more **Brandy** <term>Brandewijn</term> <note>Literally "burnt wine," the general term for distilled spirits.</note> can be drawn than from **Rye** or **Wheat**; likewise from the coarse **horse beans** <note>Also known as broad beans or fava beans.</note>; for these give off such a great spirit that the grain does not even come close to it; yet these must be handled just as I have taught in **The Golden Lion** <note>Likely a reference to a preceding chapter or a related volume in this series of alchemical/distillation instructions.</note> regarding the **Rye** and **Wheat**; so that they must first sprout <note>The malting process where seeds begin to germinate to convert starches to sugars.</note>, then be dried and crushed or ground, before one puts them into the **distillation kettle**.

<margin>/</margin>**Oats** also give a good **Brandy**, and must be handled in the same way, as one can also read there.

<margin>/</margin>From dried **Raisins** and **Plums**, as well as from **Syrup** and **Honey**, one can also obtain a **Spirit of Wine** <term>Wijn-geest</term> <note>The alchemical term for ethanol or high-proof alcohol.</note>; when these are placed in common water, and then a **ferment** or **leaven** <note>original: "suurdeeg" (sourdough); used here as a general term for a fermentation starter.</note> is added, it begins to work; and when one notices that it has finished working, then it is time to put the same into the **distillation kettle**: but beforehand, whatever one wishes to prepare must always be placed in hot or warm water, so that it can begin to work; and one shall keep it warm until it has completed its work. **Syrup** boiled with **Hops**, and set in the ferment with a **starter** <term>gift</term> <note>In historical brewing, "gift" refers to the addition or "pitching" of yeast to begin fermentation.</note>, gives a drink like beer,

# or the Salt of the Philosophers.

<header>...pleasantly and sweetly flavored.</header> The same can also be done with the **Pear-syrup**, of which more is told further below: one takes a quarter-pound of **hops**, and 10 pounds of **Syrup**, to 60 cans of water.

<margin>]</margin>One can make a **Wine** for drinking from **Raspberries**, **Hawthorn-apples**, sweet **white Plums**, **Morello cherries**, and **Cherries**; and from the same one can also draw **Brandy** <term>Brandewijn</term> <note>Literally "burnt wine," refers to distilled spirits.</note>, which far surpasses **Grain-brandy**, for the latter does not have such a good aroma of itself as the former does.

<margin>]</margin>From the **yeast** <term>gift</term> <note>Also known as barm or the frothy head of fermenting beer.</note> of the **Beer**, as well as from the **spent grain** <term>draf</term> <note>The solid residue left after the mashing of grains for brewing.</note> from which the **Beer** is boiled, one can still distill good **Brandy**, if one handles the same well to bring it into **ferment**; though when it is cold, everything must stand a little longer.

<margin>]</margin>From the **Sugar-pears**, a juice is pressed in Germany as if it were **Syrup**, so thick and so sweet, which they call **Beren-kruid** <note>original: "Beren-kruid" (Birnenkraut), a traditional thick, dark fruit reduction or pear butter still produced in parts of Germany and the Low Countries.</note> or **Pear-syrup**; this same substance, placed in warm clean water with a **ferment** of **leaven** <note>original: "suurdeeg" (sourdough).</note> or **yeast** mixed into it, then also begins to work; and from that a very delicious **Brandy** can be made, which will be found much better than any other.

One can also, by means of a **philosophical lye** <note>original: *Philofophifche looge*</note> made from wood-ashes, remove the green and foul taste from this **domestic tobacco** <note>original: *Inlandfchen Toebak*</note>, so that it shall then be better than any foreign variety; and if the tobacco is not soaked <note>original: *gefouten*, likely meaning treated or steeped.</note> with this lye, one shall never be able to remove the bad taste from it;

### Or the Salt of the Philosophers

gives, from which an earthen vessel can be made, but little matter from which gold is made. From this it is then surely to be seen that **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉; in alchemy, this symbol represents both the Sun and the metal gold.</note> must be brought forth from a particular mineral, from which the **Stone of the Wise** <note>Also known as the Philosophers' Stone, the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold.</note> comes. Of such matter **Job** speaks in chapter 28, verse 2, when he says: *Iron is taken out of the dust.* <note>The author uses the Dutch word *stof* (dust or matter); most English Bibles translate this as "earth."</note> These reasons are so clear in themselves that they need no further explanation. Therefore the famous Philosopher **Basil** <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the early modern period.</note> speaks: *Whoever has no dust and ashes can make no Metallic Salt.* From this one can easily see that there must necessarily be another matter than that of the common molten metals: for everything that has passed through the **Fire** <note>symbol: Δ; the upward-pointing triangle is the alchemical shorthand for the element of fire.</note> has lost its original nature. So it is with all things in the world. Therefore, be warned by me as a friend, that in my opinion the **Gold of the Wise** must never have been in the **Fire**, and so it can also be no common **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note>: but it must necessarily be a moist vapor, just as one reads in the Book of **Genesis**, chapter 2, verse 6, that *a mist went up from the earth and watered the whole face of the ground.* This distilled vapor is nothing other than the **Mercury of the Wise** <note>symbol: ☿; "Philosophical Mercury" is not common quicksilver, but a refined, spiritualized substance considered the "mother" of all metals.</note>, which, drawn up by the rays of the Sun and the Moon, is <insert>coagulated</insert> <term>coagulated|The process of thickening or solidifying; in alchemy, this refers to a spirit or vapor becoming a physical body.</term> in the earth—

is taught, and afterward distributed to all the fruits of the Land; which pressed-out Matter I have called the **Vitriol of the earth** in the *Golden Lion* <note>Likely a reference to another work or a preceding chapter by the same author.</note>, about which you can read more extensively on pages 1, 2, and 3.

**Pythagoras**, in the *Turba of the Wise* <note>original: *Turba der Wyſen*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts, which presents a debate among pre-Socratic philosophers.</note> page 550, speaks thus: *The Philosophers say that their Stone is found in all places, yes, upon the mountains, and in the caves.* Page 551, he also says; *that their Stone grows in two Mountain Ranges, such as in the Heaven, and in the earth.* He says also, *that this Stone is to be found everywhere, and that they have therefore given it many names; for this Stone is everywhere, throughout the whole World.* Therefore the **Sun** <note>symbol: ☉; in alchemy, the Sun represents the active, masculine principle and is often synonymous with the perfection of Gold.</note> speaks; *I am the Stone, or in me you shall seek it.* And the Philosophers say further, *that the work is nothing else than **Women’s-work and Children’s-play** <note>A famous alchemical paradox: the "Great Work" is described as "women's work" (like washing or cooking) and "children's play" to suggest that while the secret is hard to find, the actual process is simple and uses common materials.</note>: yet this Women’s-work and Children’s-play is sometimes actually the earth, and then also the <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿; the "Mercury of the Philosophers" is the fluid, transformative principle of the Matter.</note>.*

One could well write a whole book about these reasons, for those to whom this Women’s-work and Children’s-play is known. Do children not love to play with **Fire** <note>symbol: Δ</note>; and do the women not constantly use **Water** <note>symbol: ∇</note> on all sides? And herein is surely the Matter of the Wise. I say then further that whoever cannot make **Fire** <note>symbol: Δ</note> from the **Water** <note>symbol: ∇</note>, shall not come to see the Matter of the Wise Philosophers...

to extract: and then I give as an answer, that the same must not be sought from common Gold, but from that which has been driven into the earth by Nature, and is hatched by the Sun. Is the Earth not a perfect body? Yes; if the Earth were not perfect, then it would not be able to bring forth anything perfect, just as we can see daily in all the fruits of the field.

This Table is brought here from Raymond <note>Raymond Lull (c. 1232–1315), a major figure in medieval philosophy; many alchemical texts were attributed to him.</note> and other Philosophers, so that you might see how from one come two, and two give three, from which the one comes forth <note>This is a reference to the Axiom of Maria Prophetissa, a famous alchemical dictum regarding the stages of the Great Work.</note>. # ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

Silver and Gold are held by the common man to be two very great lights; like the Sun for Gold, and the Moon for Silver; and yet this is not what is meant by the Philosophers; because they expressly say that their Silver is not the common [metal], and that their Gold has never been in the fire <note>original symbol: △, meaning fire; the author is distinguishing between metallic gold and "Philosophical Gold," which has not been subjected to a common furnace.</note>.

Furthermore, the Ancient Sages call their Gold *Sol* <note>Latin: Sun</note>, and also *Sal* <note>Latin: Salt</note>, or Salt, and also Sulfur; and these are all nothing but one and the same thing. They also call it Earth and Water, drawn from their Magnet: and in what manner we shall obtain that Magnet, *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist.</note> teaches us in the *Manual on the Philosophers' Stone* <note>original title: "Manuale de Lapide Philosophorum"</note>, page 422, with the following words: *The forging of the Philosophers' Stone <note>original: "Lapidem Philosophorum"</note> through Vulcan <note>The Roman god of fire, here representing the alchemical fire or the art of smelting.</note> occurs in this manner; that through Vulcan, that Stone shall first be extracted in the fire.*

From this it is easy enough to understand that the Matter of the Sages is prepared through fire <note>original symbol: △</note>; for the Matter itself is a fire <note>original symbol: △</note>, and a water <note>original symbol: ▽</note> that does not make the hands wet <note>A classic alchemical riddle referring to "Philosophical Mercury," a substance that acts like a liquid but does not possess the physical properties of common water.</note>. All the friends of this glorious art must know this: that Nature does not make the Stone <note>original: "Lapidem"</note>, but only the Matter from which we can obtain it; which I call the soul of the earth, or fire <note>original symbol: △</note> and water <note>original symbol: ▽</note> alike, [brought] forth from a mineral...

# ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

<image-desc>An engraving depicting a tiered, square-based obelisk in a landscape with low hills and a few bare trees. The obelisk is topped with the alchemical symbol for Mercury (☿).
