->One makes from Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> a glass by itself <note>original Latin: *Vitrum per se*, referring to a substance fused into a glass-like state without any added flux or auxiliary ingredients.</note>,<-
->in the following manner.<-

<margin>Basilius <note>Likely referring to Basil Valentine, the legendary 15th-century alchemist credited with many works on antimony.</note></margin>
<margin>K. Digby <note>Sir Kenelm Digby (1603–1665), a famous English courtier and natural philosopher known for his "powder of sympathy" and alchemical experiments.</note></margin>
**Take Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿. In the context of "Glass" and "Basilius," this often refers to Antimony (Stibnite), which was frequently called the "Mercury of the Sages" or "Grey Wolf."</note>.** This shall be crushed small, and then sifted through a sieve. Thereafter one must take an iron plate, and it shall be placed upon a furnace with a small fire <note>original symbol: △</note>; and then one shall lay the pulverized Mercury upon it somewhat thinly, and constantly stir the same with an iron spatula, so that it may dry gently. Note Well: If you apply too strong a fire <note>original symbol: △</note> under your iron plate, then the Mercury would run into small beads <note>The author warns that excessive heat will cause the powder to melt back into metallic droplets, ruining the calcination process.</note>, and thus the Matter would be spoiled, and no good glass could come from it: For Mercury melts very easily.

# The Red Lion

...with a small fire. Therefore be careful in the calcination <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures in air to remove volatile substances or to oxidize it.</note> of this Matter, so that it does not spoil. When your <term>Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁. Known as stibnite in its mineral form, it was a central material in the search for the Philosopher's Stone.</note></term> has become well dried—which can happen in a short time—then take a clean crucible and put your calcined Antimony into it. Place it in the <term>fire <note>original symbol: Δ</note></term>, and let it melt gently until it becomes a black mass or oil. 

When you notice this, you must stick an iron wire into the crucible; thus you will be able to see by the Matter that remains hanging on the iron whether it is red on the inside. And when you perceive that, take your crucible quickly out of the fire and pour it out very thinly onto a <term>copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note></term> plate. 

**Note:** You must first make the copper plate warm or hot before you pour the molten Matter onto it. When it is then cold, it will be transparently red on the inside, but black on the outside.

This is the true glass of Antimony, which I made in the year 1668. One must be well-experienced in the manual operations, or else it will fail you, as also happened to me at first; yet without losses <note>original Dutch: "schaden," referring here to the loss of materials or failed attempts common in laboratory learning.</note>, one can learn nothing.

<image-desc>A curly bracket on the left side groups the lines of the following final paragraph.</image-desc>
From this Glass of Antimony, a fine Red Soul <note>In alchemical theory, the "soul" (anima) refers to the essential tincture or the internal "sulfur" of the mineral that gives it its medicinal or transformative power.</note> can be drawn by means of a vinegar made therefrom; the preparation of the same follows as such.

->...or the Salt of the Philosophers.<-

### Vinegar of Antimony by itself.

<margin>Basil Valentine <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, supposedly a Benedictine monk, whose writings on Antimony were highly influential.</note></margin>
<margin>K. Digby <note>Sir Kenelm Digby (1603–1665), an English courtier and natural philosopher known for his "sympathetic powder" and alchemical experiments.</note></margin>
Take: Mineral Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁. The raw ore, stibnite.</note>. This shall be pounded small and put into a glass with a narrow neck. Upon this, one shall then pour rainwater <note>original symbol: ♒︎. Rainwater was often preferred by alchemists for its perceived purity and "heavenly" influence.</note>, three times as much as the Antimony weighs. This water should be collected in the month of March <note>Alchemists believed March rainwater contained a special "universal spirit" linked to the start of the astrological year.</note>. When the water has stood upon the Antimony for four or five weeks, then you shall pour off that water through a felt paper and place it for a second time upon fresh Antimony, which you shall draw off *by distillation* <note>original: per diſtillationem</note>, and thereafter put it again upon fresh Antimony, until finally the water is as sour as vinegar. Note: The more often this vinegar-like *Water* <note>original: Aqua</note> is placed on fresh Antimony, the stronger you shall obtain it.

When you have your solvent <note>original symbol: ⚹. While this symbol often represents Sal Ammoniac, the context here refers to the acidic "Vinegar of Antimony" just prepared.</note> ready, then smash the Glass of Antimony <note>The red, transparent glass prepared on the previous page.</note> into pieces, which upon pulverizing shall be pure red. Put that then into your prepared solvent, and let it stand there for three months; then the vinegar shall have become entirely red, which you shall then distill <note>In this context, perhaps meaning to filter or refine; original: diſtilleren.</note> through a paper. Upon the remainder of the Matter, more of the other solvent shall be added as before, which you must likewise let stand for three months, and so this vinegar shall again be colored like the first. This pouring-on shall be done until all the *Soul* <note>original: Anima. In alchemy, the "soul" refers to the colored essence or tincture of a substance, believed to hold its medicinal and transformative power.</note>

<sig>G 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: of</meta>

<summary>This page describes the preparation of "Vinegar of Antimony," a powerful solvent made by repeatedly distilling rainwater over antimony ore. This solvent is then used to extract a red "Soul" or tincture from the Glass of Antimony over several months.</summary>
<keywords>Vinegar of Antimony, Basil Valentine, Kenelm Digby, solvent, rainwater, distillation, Anima, extraction, laboratory practice</keywords>

has been extracted from the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ☿. When you then have all of your <term>Sal ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: 🜹. A salt used as a powerful solvent in alchemical preparations.</note> together in a glass, then set a helm <note>The cap of a distillation apparatus.</note> upon it, and distill off the moisture with a gentle <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>, so you shall find a black, dark mass at the bottom, which you may then take, and pour upon it one <term>Pound</term> <note>original symbol: ♎︎</note> of good Rhenish brandy, so the spirit of wine <note>Alcohol</note> shall become red in color; which you shall then filter through a paper and treat in the same way as was taught regarding the <term>Sal ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: 🜹</note>; drawing off the brandy by common distillation, there remains *at the bottom* <note>original: in fondo</note> the medicine against all kinds of fevers: For this is the true *Soul* <note>original: Anima</note> of or from the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, whose virtues one cannot describe with any pen. At that time now, my housewife had taken only two grains, and those worked even more than the 10 or 12 grains did before. From this I have found this reason: that in winter, medicines work more in a person than in summer: for in summer, the stomach of a person is cold inside,

### To make *Cinnabar* out of *Mercury* and *Jupiter*.
<note>In alchemy, Jupiter is the planetary name for the metal Tin. Cinnabar (mercury sulfide) is a bright red mineral used as a pigment.</note>

In "The Golden Lion" <note>likely a reference to a specific alchemical text or a previous chapter titled as such</note> it is taught how one can make *Mosaic Gold* <note>original: *Aurum Musicum*; a golden-colored pigment made of tin sulfide</note>; yet nothing was said there concerning the *Dead Head* <note>original Latin: *Caput Mortuum*; a term used by alchemists to describe the "worthless" residue or dross remaining after distillation or sublimation</note>, of which I shall now share something here with the seekers of the Art. 

When you have then made your Gold in the manner described in "The Golden Lion," it shall sit atop the *Dregs* <note>original: *Feces*; the solid remains at the bottom of a vessel</note>, which are the Cinnabar. Their color appears like iron filings, very beautiful and bright; but when it is rubbed upon a grinding stone, it has a luster like deep purple-red. This color or tincture is resistant to the air, and very useful for those who wish to paint a dark red and a fiery color, as well as for dyeing all kinds of beautiful cloths. N. B. When you make this Gold, see to it that you do not take the same out of the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> too early; for if you do that, then instead of Gold you shall find a yellow powder

Take <term>℞: Recipe / Take</term> 6 pounds of White Antimony <term>♁: antimony</term>. This shall be placed in an earthen jug or pot, which one shall cover at the top so that no filth may fall into it. This shall then be placed on a moderate fire <term>Δ: fire</term> until the antimony <term>♁: antimony</term> is well glowed through: and then it shall be kept so for 4 or 5 hours, until all the moisture is gone. Afterward, you shall let it cool and take your calcined <note>calcination is the process of heating a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to remove volatile parts or change its chemical state</note> antimony <term>♁: antimony</term> out of the pot, which you shall crush small in a mortar into a fine powder. # The Red Lion,

# ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

[Paracelsus] shows us that in all these species there is a volatile spirit, which does not yet remain constant in its testing <note>original: "Examen"; referring to the alchemical trial or analysis of a substance's stability.</note>. Regarding the practical techniques <note>original: "handgrepen"; literally 'hand-grips', meaning the manual operations or methods of the laboratory.</note> concerning this, he writes in his book called *Archidoxis* <note>The *Archidoxis magica* is one of Paracelsus's most influential works, detailing the extraction of "mysteries" or essences from natural things.</note>, so that no broader explanation is necessary; especially since it was already recounted here previously in what manner the Pure is separated from the Impure; and that happens through nothing other than Distillation, Calcination, and Sublimation, of which other books are full. This I wanted to share with the friends of glorious Alchemy, to warn them that if they wish to make any medicines from the metals, they can also prepare them from minerals and marcasites <note>A historical term for various metallic sulfide minerals, often iron pyrites, believed to contain the "seed" of metals.</note>, without needing to use the actual metals for that purpose.

# The Red Lion,
<meta>catchword: This</meta>

The metals that give the most signs in the leaches or water <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> are these three: Lead <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, and Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note>; but Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, and Tin <note>original symbol: ♃</note> do not have such a nature: for this sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and the salts have come forth from a <term>fixed mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; "fixed" refers to mercury that has been treated to withstand heat without evaporating.</note>, of which much has been treated in *The Golden Lion* <note>Likely a reference to another volume or chapter in this series of alchemical works.</note>.

If you now wish to know the reason why Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, and Tin <note>original symbol: ♃</note> do not show themselves so easily in the water, it is because they do not have as much Salt as Lead <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, and Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note> do; and therefore they are also not so easily dissolved by the water <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>: for common water also has a salt, which is an enemy of the salt of the metals, and can consume their salts into water, from which the metals are made.

Rhumelius <note>Johannes Pharamundus Rhumelius (1597–1661), a notable German physician and alchemist whose works circulated widely in the 17th century.</note> states on page 290 these following words: "*In the earth there are three kinds of salts: first, a volatile or fleeting salt, Sal Ammoniac, compared to mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>; the second, Niter <note>original: Nitrum; symbol: 🜔</note>; the third, Alkali salt, which is fixed and compared to common gold.*" And according to this, he further says on the same page in Chapter 9: That salt is the root and the key of the whole art of <term>Alchemy</term>. This can be observed when one melts the metals by means of the salts: for the salt is the water <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> with which the metals are washed of their impurities, of which enough has been taught in my treatise.

# or the Salt of the Philosophers.

# The Red Lion

...the ashes which, burned to dust by the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: $\Delta$</note>, are dissolved in the yellow water; thus it is produced from the white [substance], which together is Salt and Earth. And this is the white spirit of the Lime of the Philosophers.

<margin><note>Pencil bracket in margin from this line to the bottom of the page.</note></margin>And so that you may have a clear proof of this Operation, read *Basilius* in his repetition of the 12 Keys, where you shall find the following words, page 202:

Yet this Operation is also but simple <note>original: *slecht* — in this historical context, this usually means "simple," "plain," or "straightforward" rather than "bad."</note>, as far as I can perceive: for in this work one shall break no <term>retorts</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long downward-pointing neck used for distillation.</note> into pieces, nor need to buy glass <term>flasks</term> or <term>helms</term> <note>original: *helmen* — the top part of a still, also known as an alembic.</note>, since those are not at all necessary in this work: for all the Philosophers write with one accord, that the Art of wisdom does not consist in common distillation.

In *The Golden Lion* I have written about making a Red Oil from <term>Saturn</term> <note>Alchemical symbol ♄; referring to Lead</note>; and here I shall describe how one can extract a white Oil from it, as follows.

### The White Oil of <term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead</note>.

Take 1 pound of White Lead. This shall be handled in the following manner. First, you shall take good wine vinegar, which is entirely unadulterated; in this you shall put sublimated <term>sal-armoniac</term> <note>original symbol: 🜹; ammonium chloride</note>, or a good *Spirit* of Urine <note>Ammonia</note>, in equal parts: but one shall then add as much <term>sal-armoniac</term> into the <term>vinegar</term> <note>original symbol: 🜊</note> as it will dissolve. This shall then be put in a glass flask, above which one shall hang <term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead</note> beaten as thin as paper. When that Lead hangs above the <term>vinegar</term>, then that flask must be covered at the top with a stone or a piece of glass. This shall then be placed where it has a very gentle warmth, just like a hen brooding her eggs; and so it shall remain standing for three weeks, then you shall finally see the Lead covered in a calx <note>A powder resulting from the process; original: *Kalk*</note>, which you shall scrape off, and hang the <term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead</note> once again over that vinegar, until all the <term>Saturn</term> is changed into a <term>powder</term> <note>original symbol: r, likely denoting a calx or residue</note>. This calx shall then be dissolved with <term>vinegar</term>, which one shall let distill <note>filter</note> through a felt paper; thereafter one shall let it evaporate again on a gentle <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>,

### How one shall prepare the Oil of Talc.

Bringing the Talc or the Mother-of-Pearl to an Oil happens thus.
℞. Mother-of-Pearl: this shall be pounded small, and then calcined <note>To heat a substance to a high temperature to remove volatile substances or reduce it to a powder</note> in a gentle fire <note>The symbol Δ is used here to represent "Fire"</note>, until everything is as white as snow, and as light as dust. This shall then be laid in a clean cellar, which is also somewhat damp, so this calcined Talc draws the air to itself through its magnet <note>Alchemists believed certain prepared minerals acted as "magnets" to pull a "universal spirit" or "nitre" from the atmosphere; scientifically, this describes deliquescence, where a salt absorbs moisture until it dissolves</note>; which you shall then notice when you first weigh the dry powder before you lay it in the cellar; and when it has stood there for 3 or 4 days, weigh it again, and you shall find that it has become much heavier; and this happens through the air, which mixes itself invisibly with the same and increases it. This Powder that you have then taken out of the cellar can be distilled through a <term>Retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, curved neck used for distilling liquids</note>, so there comes over a white spirit or fire <note>original: "witten geeft of Δ"</note>, which is very little, and this you shall keep. Thereafter you shall take that <term>Caput mortuum</term> <note>Latin for "dead head"; the solid residue left over after distillation</note>, and that shall yet again, through a <term>Reverberation</term> <note>Heating in a furnace where the flame is reflected or "reverberated" onto the substance</note>, be made magnetic, and then as before brought into the cellar upon a glass Table:

*Rhumelius* says that the Talc, as he teaches to make it, shall crystallize as if it were Saltpeter, in which he shall also not fail. # The Red Lion,
<meta>catchword: are</meta>

cannot be obtained, except by investigating Nature; for he who does not seek, shall arrive at no knowledge.

<margin>Diagonal pencil lines alongside paragraphs 2 and 3.</margin>
The Art of the Philosophers consists in three things: namely, Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury; and these are the principal parts in the Art: yet I do not mean common Mercury, nor common Salt <note>original symbol: ⊖</note> and Sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>, but those which have been produced by Nature in one Mother through the power of God.

If then the first Matter of the Metals, according to the sayings of the Ancient Sages, is Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, Sulfur, and Salt, then the common mineral of Lead <note>original symbol: ♄ (Saturn)</note> cannot be that first Matter: for in Lead there is indeed a Salt; but whether it is that which the Philosophers mean, when they say of it, that the Wind carried it in its belly <note>A famous citation from the *Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus*, referring to the volatile or spiritual nature of the alchemical substance</note>, that I leave to the judgment of those who know better. The Salt of the Sages is *fixed* <note>original: *fixum*; meaning it remains stable and does not evaporate when exposed to heat</note>, and the Salt of Lead, on the contrary, is volatile. The Mercury of the Sages is also no metal that has ever been in the fire <note>original symbol: △</note>; and that which they call Sulfur is likewise stable in the fire: how then could common Lead be the first Matter of the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>The legendary goal of alchemy, believed to be a substance that could transmute base metals into gold and heal all diseases</note>, when this contradicts all their writings? Therefore, one must not take common Lead for the Matter of the Philosophers; for the Mercury of the Ancient Sages is a mineral water, just as is their Sulfur. These

<summary>The author argues that the true "First Matter" of alchemy is not found in common lead or ordinary chemicals, but in spiritualized versions of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury (the Tria Prima) that remain stable in fire.</summary>

### or the Salt of the Philosophers.

have never been in the fire <note>original symbol: Δ</note>; but their Salt is purified by the fire, and that they also call salt or Sal Ammoniac <note>original symbol: ⊕</note>, though not the common kind: for the Salt of which the Sages write has fire within itself, and is White, Red, and Yellow. *Sendivogius* calls it his Steel; *Basilius* calls it his Magnet: *Paracelsus* calls it the Heaven of Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄; referring here to lead or the "dark" initial stage of the work</note>. *Rhumelius* (page 290) calls it Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and Sal Ammoniac <note>original symbol: ⊕</note>; and furthermore they call this Matter by obscure names, and thus I shall now let it remain as it is, requesting all Friends of the Art to be satisfied with this.

One burns Wood to an Ash, and from that one obtains a Salt from which a Glass is made, and its fine red soul provides a good red Pigment; but this must be dissolved in its Mercury.

The Tartar <note>original symbol: 🜪</note> is <gloss>dissolved</gloss> <note>original: *gesolveert*</note>, calcined <note>burned to a powder</note>, and then filtered, which one then dries over a gentle fire <note>original symbol: Δ</note>, and afterwards allows to melt into an Oil in a damp cellar, which is then the true Oil of Tartar <note>original: *Wynsteen* or "Wine-stone," the crust left in wine barrels</note>. <margin><insert>See Arc: Fol:</insert></margin> This serves against Gout <note>original: *Podagra*</note>, which can then be prepared for use by a good Spirit <note>original symbol: 🝅</note> of Brandy; of which *Basilius* and *Paracelsus* have written enough, for which reason I shall now add no more concerning it.

# ->The Red Lion,<-

The Ancient Sages <note>original: "De Oude Wyfen"; literally "The Old Wives," often referring to folk wisdom or traditional teachings</note> have taught that the Metals and Minerals are brought forth by a single means in the bowels of the earth; and this same means they call **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁; here referring to the mineral principle of the earth</note>, on which I also worked previously, but could find nothing more than a good *medicine* for mankind, and not for the Metals to cure them of their diseases <note>In alchemy, "diseases" of metals refer to their impurities or their "imperfect" state compared to gold</note> as was my intention; but I failed in this, and the reason I could not perceive at that time. For I thought only of common **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, which one goes to buy from the grocers, thinking that this would be the *Salt of the Philosophers*; but it is quite otherwise: for their Salt is made from a Metal that has life, and which must be killed, and then rise once again in its *First Matter* <note>Latin: *Prima Materia*; the primordial, chaotic substance from which all things originate</note>, which is then its fine Salt. Do the *Philosophers* not all say with one accord that their *Matter* is a living Salt, a living **Sulphur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>, and a living **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, and in this the truth is spoken by them.

For suppose you wish to extract a *Mercury* from any Metals that are dead; then you must first bring them to a Salt or to an **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, from which you shall afterwards make a **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, which is still not their *First Matter*: but when your **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> has been brought forth that far, then you must kill it again.

### ->or the Salt of the Philosophers.<-

...that is, to bring it to a Salt <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, which is then but the first part of your work, in order to change the Salt into a Watery Mercury <note>original: "Aqua ☿"; refers to a liquid or 'philosophical' state of mercury</note>.

I have known and spoken with people who had made the Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> of Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, thinking to obtain the "Great Work" <note>The *Magnum Opus*, or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> therefrom; but it did not wish to succeed for them, because they could not make a Salt from their Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, and therefore they could not proceed any further with it.

I know furthermore that they melted their Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> back into Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, yet with damage and loss of their metal. Nature has completed her work in the Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>—that is, from a Salt, to a Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, and further into the Metal—from which we can make nothing other than, at the very most, an Oil <note>In alchemy, an 'oil' or 'sulfur' is an extract, but the author implies it lacks the 'living' power needed for transformation</note>.

The Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> of the Ancient Sages is made by Nature: when it is brought into a Salt, it transforms into its First Matter <note>Latin: *Prima Materia*; the original, undifferentiated substance from which all things are created</note>, but it shall never again return to its previous Body <note>Latin: *Corpus*; the physical form of the metal</note>, and this same thing I have seen with my own eyes.

I say further that he may well pass for a Master who can make common Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> in such a way that it does not return to its previous being—that is, into Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, as it is called by the common man; but he who could bring it into its First Matter <note>Latin: *Prima Materia*</note>, to him [I]...

# The Red Lion,

# The Red Lion,

...ing, I believe that a great work could be made from it. I understand it thus: that it must be brought into its three first preceding essences, namely into Salt, into Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term>, and Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term>; and I consider this its first beginning, since all these things are to be found therein, as far as is known to me.

If you wish to make such a Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term> out of common Lead <term>original symbol: ♄; Latin: Saturnus</term>, you shall not find it there at all; for God has not placed it in the Lead, and therefore one must not seek it there.

The first beginning of Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term> is water, which is clothed with a *fixed Salt* <note>Latin: *Sal fixum*; a salt that remains stable and does not evaporate when exposed to high heat</note>, and covered by a volatile <note>In alchemy, "volatile" refers to a substance that easily evaporates or escapes as vapor when heated</note> Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term>, which one calls Antimony <term>original symbol: ⊕</term>, or the spirit of Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term>. If one could capture these two, then I believe the work would well reward our efforts; but he who is concerned with it can do as I do, and diligently investigate Nature, and so he shall find therein what is right.

It is well known that when one melts minerals, they must first lose their Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term> and Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term> through the fire of common flaming wood, and that the fixed Salt remains with it, by which the Metal is then melted; for this Sulfur <term>original symbol: 🜍</term> and Mercury <term>original symbol: ☿</term> is the life of the ore or mineral. # The Red Lion,

...if God grants me life long enough, and also permits me to reveal such a mystery to the world.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In the smelting of Minerals or mountain-ores, the <term>Fire original symbol: $\triangle$</term> robs much from them, which it carries away in the smoke, through the air, back to its Fatherland <note>Likely referring to the elemental realm of air or the source from which the substance originated</note>. The Salt, which is the <term>Salt original symbol: $\oplus$</term>, hinders the Minerals during smelting; and this happens when the Salts have not been well washed away or leached out beforehand.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;If one is not well experienced in the handling of this washing, then one loses significantly from the Mineral—indeed, so much that one can notice during smelting that the water takes much of the Mineral with it. And the reason for this is that the waters are sharpened <note>made more acidic or chemically reactive</note> by the Salts, and then take the Metal with them: therefore a good Mine Master <term>original: Bergmeester; a technical official overseeing mining and smelting operations</term>, regarding all these things that might occur to him, ought to have a good Medicine <note>original: Medicyne; here referring to a chemical reagent or corrective agent</note> against this washing-away of the Metals—namely, a precipitate <term>original: nederslag</term> to kill <note>neutralize or fix</note> the Salt. I would treat these matters even more extensively; however, since I find that for all my devotion I have only received ingratitude from certain envious and malicious people, I consider it most advisable not to write more broadly about it in this Booklet.

# The Red Lion,

called [the Red Lion] of the Metals, because this Salt is united with the Spirits of the Metals, which one can still perceive by its fine scent when taken upon the tongue, where it will be found to have a bitter taste, drawing the mouth together: and this can in a short time take on the nature of a Metal.

Do not understand me to mean that I would write here of Common Salt, but of various other kinds of Salts; such as of <term>Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note></term>, <term>Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note></term>, <term>Arsenic? <note>original OCR: o-o; likely representing a mineral salt or arsenic</note></term>, <term>Salt <note>original symbol: ⊖</note></term>, <term>Vinegar? <note>original OCR: o+; possibly a symbol for distilled vinegar or a specific alkaline salt</note></term>, <term>Sulfur? <note>original OCR: Φ; likely a variant symbol for a chemical principle</note></term>, <term>Tin <note>original symbol: ♃</note></term> and so forth. These all have their beginning from the <term>Water <note>original symbol: ∇, representing the element of Water</note></term> of the Earth and of Heaven, in which everything is brought forth through the air and comes to light through the power of the Earth, with the help of the Sun and the Moon: for where the work of the Sun happens to cease, there everything is dead, and then the Moon would bring about the death of all things through the great cold that is within her.

The Ancient Sages named the Metals after the Planets of the Heavens, such as Gold for the Sun, Silver for the Moon, Iron for Mars, Copper for Venus, Tin for Jupiter, and Lead for Saturn. <term>Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note></term> has his whole number <note>A reference to Gematria or the numerical value of the name Hermes/Mercury, suggesting it contains the properties of all other metals</note> within his name, which is why the Ancients said he was "all in all"; of which *Paracelsus* speaks at length in his book *Vexations* <note>original: *Vexationum*, referring to his work *Vexationes Alchemistarum* (The Vexations of the Alchemists)</note>, page 348.

### or the Salt of the Philosophers.

<margin>K. Digby <note>Refers to Sir Kenelm Digby (1603–1665), an English courtier and philosopher renowned for his alchemical interests and "Powder of Sympathy."</note></margin>
One can indeed calcine <note>To calcine (calcineren) is to heat a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to oxidize it or change its state.</note> minerals *per se* <note>original Latin: "by itself."</note>; but before doing that, one can draw their spirit out of them. This same thing is done when one lets them dry in the Sun and then lays them at night on a flat dish or table; thus the mineral attracts the Spirit of the World <note>original: "♎ Mundi" (Spiritus Mundi). The symbol for Libra [♎] was often used to represent the balanced or universal spirit of the world.</note>, which one can then drive out through a retort <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation.</note>. The *caput mortuum* <note>Latin: "dead head," the dregs or residue remaining after a distillation or chemical reaction.</note> shall be laid in the air as before, yet it must not rain upon it, but the Dew of Heaven <note>An alchemical term for the condensed moisture of the air, believed to contain celestial influences.</note> may indeed fall upon it. This same thing can be done with all Salts; but common Mercury [☿] and Gold [☉] cannot be treated so; for they are themselves Water [▽] and Fire [△], so that they care little for common fire or the air, of which I shall treat more broadly in the future.

# or the Salt of the Philosophers.

for it is the spirit that gives power, and the bare Salt is not capable of that, because of the raw earth that is still attached to it.

To come then further to the natural Salts, I must confess that these are the *Bases* and foundations of this Art; for everything in the world consists of Salt. This is why the Ancients have constantly pointed us toward Salt. And Jesus Christ himself said to his Disciples:
> You are the Salt *of the Earth.* <note>A biblical reference (Matthew 5:13) used here to provide a spiritual and physical justification for the importance of Salt in alchemy.</note>

This symbol often represents Salt, specifically Salt Ammoniac or the "philosophical" salt extracted from metals.</note> from the Metals.

Mercury is a <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> that does not wet the hands, and a <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> that does not burn. <note>A classic alchemical paradox describing "Philosophical Mercury," which has the properties of a liquid but is actually a dry, metallic vapor or spirit.</note> Mercury is a smoke, which is carried up by common <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>. # The Red Lion, <note>Dutch: *De Roode Leeuw*; a common alchemical name for the Philosophers' Stone or a specific state of the alchemical process</note>

beings such as Salt and Sulfur; though not fixed <note>In alchemy, "fixed" refers to a substance that can withstand heat without evaporating</note>, which I believe cannot be brought forth without Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> or Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>. This is also read in the *Turba* <note>referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers," one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts</note>: and Paracelsus says further in his *Book of Vexations* <note>original Latin title: *Liber Vexationum*</note>, that Gold and Silver are very easily made from Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, Lead <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, and Tin <note>original symbol: ♃</note>, but always by adding Gold and Silver to them. However, when Paracelsus also proposes this here, of making Gold easily from Mercury, Lead, and Tin, he means by it the spirits of the Metals, and Sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and Salt <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, when those three are well purified, and then joined together after proper preparation, and that one shall then add the Gold or Silver to it, but otherwise nothing else.

Now, because I have treated even more of the Salts here previously, I shall here finally say more fully that the Salt of the Metals is the Mercury, or that one can find it within: but that one must set them together with such Metals that have the greatest correspondence with each other in Nature, like Man and Woman <note>An alchemical metaphor for the union of opposing principles, such as the "fixed" and the "volatile"</note>; and from these the Mercury of the Wise <note>original: *☿ der Wysen*; also known as Philosophical Mercury, the essential "soul" of matter sought by alchemists</note> is brought forth, which Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist</note> writes of in his treatise named the *Treatise on the Philosophers' Stone*: namely, that one shall take Man and Woman. These he also calls his mines, the one of Iron, and the other of Steel. Paracelsus calls that in his book *The Treasure of Alchemists* <note>original Latin: *Thesaurus Alchymistarum*</note>, in one place—

is a Mineral, and in another place he calls it Cinnabar-ore <note>original: *Synober-eerts*; the primary ore of mercury</note>: after that he gives it the name of the Vitriol of Venus <note>original: *Vitriool van Venus*; historically referring to copper sulfate, often used in alchemical transformations</note>. Indeed, I myself have made a <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> from the Metals that was red, white, green, and sky-blue in color, with many more other colors, which are unnecessary to relate here in greater detail.

# The Red Lion,

*The Tincture of Vitriol <note>original: *Vitriool*; a sulfate mineral used to create powerful acids</note> is so strong that it tinges all white bodies red, and all red bodies white, and that is a miracle.*

*When you shall drive this Tincture through a retort <note>a glass vessel with a long, curved neck used for distilling liquids</note>, you will see a blackness come forth: drive this again through a retort, and repeat that as many times as necessary until it becomes whitish: Go forth, do not falter in your labor, rectify <note>to purify a substance by repeating the distillation process</note> until you find the true, clear Green Lion <note>original: *Groenen Leeuw*; a symbol for a potent acidic solvent or a specific stage of purified matter</note>, and you shall see him, heavy in weight and colored in himself: it is a Tincture, transparent gold <note>original: *Aurum perspectibile*</note>, until you see the sign, and may have it from the Green Lion, which cannot be paid for with all the treasures of the Roman Lion <note>original: *Roomschen Leeuw*; likely a metaphor for the worldly wealth or power of the Church or the Empire</note>. Blessed is he who finds the same and can use the Tincture.*

*This is the true balsamic oil, a balsam of the heavenly stars, which allows no bodies to perish, and allows no infection, no leprosy <note>original: *Lasery*</note>, no gout <note>original: *Podagra*; a painful form of inflammatory arthritis</note>, and no dropsy <note>original: *Water-zucht*; a historical term for swelling or edema caused by fluid retention</note> to take root; just a grain's weight administered, when it is fermented with Sulfur from Gold.*

# or the Salt of the Philosophers.

Ah! you German Charles <note>Likely a reference to Holy Roman Emperor Charles V, often addressed in alchemical polemics of the era</note>, where is your treasure, where are your naturalists <note>original: *Natuur-kenners*</note>, where are your physicians? Slugs on the wood, who only purge and lax! Thus has your heaven been torn asunder, your stars wandered, and moved along another path outside the line of well-being, while your eyes stand dazed by carbuncles <note>a gemstone, but here used metaphorically for superficial or deceptive brilliance</note> and other things, which go toward the beholding of elegance and beauty, and serve pride. If your physicians had known Prince *Galen* <note>original: *Galenum*; Claudius Galenus, the 2nd-century Roman physician whose theories dominated medicine until the Renaissance</note> (as they call him) to be in Hell, from where he has written to me, then they would have blessed themselves with a fox-tail <note>an idiom suggesting they were being fooled or were performing empty rituals</note>; as also *Avicenna* <note>original: *Avicennam*; a Persian polymath whose "Canon of Medicine" was a standard textbook for centuries</note> in the gates of Limbo <note>original: *Voorhel*</note>, with whom I would have disputed concerning his *Aurum potabile* <note>Latin: "potable gold"; a legendary alchemical medicine believed to grant longevity</note>, or drinkable gold, the Tincture of the naturalists, *Quinta Essentia* <note>Latin: "Fifth Essence"; the purest, most concentrated medicinal force of a substance</note>, or fifth being, of his Philosopher's Stone, *Mithridate* <note>original: *Mithridaet*; a complex semi-mythical remedy against all poisons</note> and *Theriac* <note>original: *Thriaec*; an ancient herbal concoction used as a universal antidote</note>, etc. Oh! what seductive hypocrites and deceivers they are; where they [hide] the truth of an upright physician, who is instructed by Nature

# The Red Lion,

*...and what is born of* **God** *therein, they despise.* <note>This sentence fragment completes a thought from the previous page, likely referring to the divine spark or "light" found within natural substances which the author’s rivals ignore.</note>

*Now behold, you* **Deceivers**, *you who sit in the high esteem of rank; after my death, my* **Disciples** *shall come and bring you* **Hypocrites** *to light, along with your filth-mixing* **Apothecaries** <term>Apothecaries: Here the author uses the derogatory "filth-mixing" to describe traditional pharmacists who created complex herbal concoctions (polypharmacy), which alchemists believed were impure and toxic compared to their own "distilled" essences.</term>, *by whom the* **Princes** *and most powerful* **Potentates** *of* **Christendom** *have been brought to their deaths.*

**Oh! Woe** *unto your necks on the day of judgment. Yet I know that the* **Supremacy** *shall be mine, and that the honor shall belong to me: not that I boast: but* **Nature** *praises me; I am born of her very self, and I follow her: she knows me, and I know her too. ## CONCLUSION.
<note>original: *Sluit-reden* — literally "Closing Reason" or "Concluding Logic"</note>

The reason why I have wished to bring this treatise <term>Tractaet — a formal written work on a specific subject</term> into the light once more is because in my two previous books—namely *The Cabinet of Minerals* <note>original: *Cabinet der Mineralen*</note> and *The Golden Lion, or the Vinegar of the Sages* <note>original: *Goude Leeuw, of den Asyn der Wysen*</note>—various matters are described that were not fully worked out. This was because something was still lacking, either in my reflections or in my experiments; I have since become more certain of these things and now wish to make them public to the world for the benefit of my fellow man and for the greater completion of my entire work. I trust that it will be found pleasing and can be used to the glory of God and for the general utility.

Here, then, all friends and lovers of this glorious Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy or Hermetic philosophy.</note> may be well-warned that no philosophical books shall be found where the Matter <term>Materie — the raw material or "First Matter" used in alchemy</term> of the Great Work <term>Groote werk — the *Magnum Opus*, the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term> is presented in such clear and explicit words. Therefore, the favorable readers must often excuse my hesitant frankness. Indeed, I even say sometimes that it is impossible to find that out through reading, when

This following final Chapter, which is *Basilius’* **Conclusion** <note>original: *Sluit-reden* — a final logical argument or summary often found at the end of a philosophical treatise.</note>, concerning the first and second parts of his **Mining Treatises** <note>original: *Bergstukken* — literally "mountain pieces," referring to his writings on mining, geology, and the subterranean world.</note>, wherein he has gathered the entire Nature of the Metals and Minerals together into one body, omitting nothing from it, except only the style and the method of working it. Thus *Basilius* continues his discourse, after his thirteenth Chapter, page 154.

## Concerning the Essence and the Nature of Gold, which is found abundantly not only in the Metals, but also in some Minerals, even as in two Metals, and reveals itself explicitly and powerfully in virtues and powers over all natures, added here as a brief appendix and conclusion to my first and second Parts of Mining Matters, Metals, and Minerals.

# The Red Lion,

->or the Salt of the Philosophers.<-

In short, this Salt is everything of which the Wise Men write.

The <term>Antimony</term> <note>Symbol: ⊕; here referring to the mineral salt serving as the "earthly" base or prime matter.</note> is indeed a Salt, but it does not have the power to work alone within the Metals without the <term>Lion of the World</term> <note>original: ♌ *Mundi*; the animating spirit or "Philosophic Mercury" believed to be the vital life-force of the universe.</note>: for it is a living Salt, Gold, and Silver, of which <term>Sendivogius</term> <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist known for his theories on the "food of life" found in the air.</note> writes, saying that his <term>Sun</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; representing Gold.</note> and <term>Moon</term> <note>original symbol: ☽; representing Silver.</note> are not common.

One calcines the Metals by means of Salts, and then one shall find a mixed species. For this reason, one must be careful not to do this with <term>Corrosive Salts</term> <note>original: *Corrofyf*; harsh acids or salts that destroy the "life" of the metal rather than purifying it.</note>, but rather with the spirits, which are then not Corrosive, but are purified bodies. For when the spirit is separated from its body, it still carries the impurity with it. You may believe this for certain, as I have clearly experienced it myself, a matter which has been treated in various places in this Book. The spirit has a body, but it is neither perceptible nor tangible; yet if you succeed in making it perceptible, you shall clearly find that I have written the truth here: and this is achieved through a <term>Philosophical distillation</term> <note>A specialized alchemical process intended to extract the essence or "spirit" of a substance without destroying its inner virtue.</note>.

For this reason, I now advise all friends that they should only seek after the spirits of the Metals, and in their Salts; for I believe the true Matter to be there. ### ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

Common <term>Antimony</term> <note>Symbol: ♁.</note> is very volatile <note>Meaning it evaporates or reacts easily when heated.</note>, but by a Salt it can be greatly constrained, so that it no longer burns away: for <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>Symbol: 🜨; also known as Nitre.</note> makes things volatile, and in part also fixed <note>In alchemy, "fixity" is the ability of a substance to resist the transformative power of fire.</note>. <margin>Note Well.</margin>
I myself could not at first believe that Antimony and Saltpeter would set one another on fire without the dust of Charcoal; therefore I put equal parts <note>original: "ana."; a term used in prescriptions meaning an equal amount of each ingredient.</note> of Saltpeter and Antimony in a flask, with its cap <note>original: "helm"; the top part of a still or alembic used for distillation.</note> upon it, and a receiver attached in the common manner, so as to obtain the spirit of the Antimony and Saltpeter. This continued until all the excessive moisture was gone, and then I necessarily had to apply a stronger <term>Fire</term> <note>Symbol: △.</note>, if I wished to have the spirits. When the glass was then very warm, there began that

-> ### or the Salt of the Philosophers. <-

The Master of Nature brought me, <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔; here representing Saltpeter or the "Universal Salt" of the alchemists.</note>, forth in Heaven and sent me into the earth, where I had to take on a body that came to meet me. If I come to the good, then I make friendship with them for eternity: if you provoke me or strike me with sticks, then I spit fire and flame <note>A reference to the explosive power of Saltpeter when used in gunpowder.</note>, yet <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Symbol: ♃; the alchemical metal Tin.</note> can tame me; but when he comes with his brother, then I ensure they obtain good peace.

Am I not a <term>Salt Ammoniac</term> <note>A volatile salt (ammonium chloride) highly valued in alchemical sublimation.</note>? Am I not found in dark cellars upon the walls and stones? Do you not find me in the stables of beasts? Yes, there I am bred and brought forth <note>Saltpeter was historically harvested from the white crusts (efflorescence) that formed on the walls of damp cellars and stables where nitrogen-rich organic matter decomposed.</note>, and no man shall live without me, nor trees, herbs, metals, and minerals; yes, all salts have their life from me: therefore I am the first and the last being, through which the whole world shall burn, and a new structure be made again.

Is there indeed any salt in the world that could be my equal? What then do you want from me, when you humans misuse me so greatly? I am compared to <term>Saturn</term> <note>Symbol: ♄; the alchemical metal Lead.</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿; Quicksilver.</note> is my internal spirit. All species are [subject] to me

<sig>O 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: subject</meta>

<summary>This page features the personified Salt of the Philosophers (Saltpeter) describing its heavenly origin, its omnipresence in all things, and its natural occurrence in cellars and stables. It warns of its destructive power when provoked (gunpowder) while asserting its vital role as the "first and last being" necessary for all life and the eventual renewal of the world.</summary>
<keywords>Saltpeter, Salt of the Philosophers, Jupiter (Tin), Saturn (Lead), Mercury, Sal Armoniac, alchemy, gunpowder, elemental personification</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the personification of the "Salt of the Philosophers" (Saltpeter), following the previous page's description of its presence in cellars and its relationship to the metals Saturn and Mercury.</meta>

-> # The Red Lion, <-

submissive. In the Winter I am gray; in the Spring I come forth with flowers, from which the fruits then sprout. The messengers who live in my house are Lightning and Thunder, which make everything tremble. I <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔; in this context, the "Universal Salt" or Saltpeter, personified here as the "Red Lion."</note> am a Sun and Moon of the earthly Salts; through my spirit they are bred and brought to light, and through me Nature gives them her Crystal: for I am their life, and also remain with them until the end of death. In me are all colors or pigments that one could imagine, according to the way that I am decomposed. My spirit is a swift Bird of the night; and *Hermes* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> says that I am the strongest of all strengths, that the wind has carried in its belly <note>A reference to the *Emerald Tablet*, a foundational alchemical text: "The wind carries it in its belly."</note>. I <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔</note> speak all this not just of myself, but of the spirit that lives in me, and which God has planted in me with His finger; so that I could perform everything that is assigned to me. Therefore *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a famous 15th-century alchemist known for his works on antimony and salts.</note> has made a poem of praise in my honor, which he has presented to you with these words, saying on page 143.

> *I am on earth a wondrous Salt,*
> *And my equal is nowhere to be seen,*
> *Since one can accomplish almost nothing without me,*
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;*Yes, I must help clarify everything here.*
> *The Eagle <note>The "Eagle" in alchemy often symbolizes the volatile spirit or the process of sublimation.</note> cannot do without me at all,*

--- Page 253 ---
<meta>This page continues the personification of the "Salt of the Philosophers," specifically referencing a poem by the alchemist Basil Valentine to explain the difference between common salt and the spiritual essence required for the alchemical Great Work.</meta>

-> ### or the Salt of the Philosophers. <-

*When those Metals intend to tinge.* <note>To "tinge" in alchemy means to transform or dye a base metal into gold or silver using the Philosopher's Stone.</note>
*Common Salt can finish nothing without me,*
*Even if it wished to turn away from me entirely.*
*A clear Ice is my entire form,*
*Wherein a hellish spirit resides;* <note>In alchemical prose, "hellish" often refers to a fiery, acidic, or highly reactive volatile spirit.</note>
*In both of which Nature*
*Comes to show herself in many kinds of Figure.*

Basilius <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 17th century.</note> speaks the truth here: for everything is, according to the will of God, brought forth through this Salt; and without this Salt no thing shall retain life. Here some might think that I spoke of the <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔; common table salt.</note>, which one sells by the pound in the shops; certainly not: but I write only of its spirit, which lives in that Body, which is the Bride for whom work is done, so that one might please her, and catch her in the net of the Metals, which must be made through the Art <note>The "Art" is a standard term for the practice of alchemy.</note>, without the addition of any foreign things that would be against the nature of this spirit; for otherwise you would not be able to tame it, for it is old and cunning: and you must also guard yourself against its breath that comes out of it; for this is a very poisonous dragon, which gives off nothing but fire and flame. Thus much be said then of the <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔</note>; and further you can go to its other companions, such as <term>Copper</term> <note>Symbol: ♀</note>, <term>Iron</term> <note>Symbol: ♂</note>, <term>Sulfur</term> <note>Symbol: 🜍</note>, <term>Vitriol</term> <note>Symbol: 🜓</note>, <term>Alum</term> <note>Symbol: 🜛</note>, <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>Symbol: 🜩</note>, <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: 🜔</note>, and <term>Nitre</term> <note>Symbol: 🜫; also known as saltpeter.</note>, and these shall give testi-

O 3
<meta>catchword: -mony ,</meta>

<summary>This page clarifies that the "Philosophic Salt" is not common table salt but a potent spiritual essence or "spirit" within matter. It warns the alchemist that this spirit is a dangerous, "poisonous dragon" that must be tamed using the proper alchemical Art without foreign impurities.</summary>
<keywords>Salt of the Philosophers, Basil Valentine, Tingeing, Alchemical Art, Dragon, Metal Symbols, Vitriol, Nitre</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from the previous page's discussion regarding the "spirit" of the Salt of the Philosophers and how it differs from common materials. The final word of the previous page "su..." likely completes into "su-ven" (the Dutch word 'geven', meaning 'to give').</meta>

# The Red Lion,

...give, [to see] whether I have not written the truth herein.

<margin>The Golden Chain <note>original: "aurea Catena." This refers to the "Golden Chain of Homer," a concept used by alchemists to describe the sequence of nature and the link between the celestial and terrestrial worlds.</note></margin>
I am still acquainted with several ways of preparation regarding <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>, which I do not wish to reveal, in order to prevent the evil that could be brought about by them. And since I have spoken of its virtues, I must also say that without it, the Metals cannot be properly opened, neither in the philosophical manner, nor according to contemporary use. For example: although you drive a spirit out of <term>Common Salt</term> <note>Symbol: ⊝; likely Sal Commune or Nitre here.</note>, it will nevertheless not do what <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note> does; for by nature there is no power in it to open the Metals and Minerals. And if you furthermore take <term>the crude matter</term> <note>Symbol: ○; possibly referring to Vitriol or Alum.</note>, and through distillation drive a <term>Spirit</term> <note>Symbol: ♎; used here as a shorthand for *Spiritus*.</note> out of it, it will still accomplish nothing in the Metals; for to that <term>crude matter</term> <note>Symbol: ○</note> also not so much is granted. Would <term>Antimony</term> <note>Symbol: ♁</note> then do the same? It is also not capable of so much; for it is for the most part of the nature of <term>Fire</term> <note>Symbol: △</note>: and since all <term>Fires</term> <note>Symbols: △ △</note> are great enemies of the Metals, they must not be used in them for the "opening" of the Art. If you wish to take <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>Symbol: ⚹</note>, and obtain a fine spirit through sublimation or fermentation, you will achieve nothing with this <term>Spirit</term> <note>Symbol: ♎</note>. When one took the spirit of <term>Antimony</term> <note>Symbol: ♁</note>, and intended therewith alone [to do] something in the Metals—

<meta>catchword: to (Dutch: 'te')</meta>

<summary>This page argues that only the "Red Lion" (a specific preparation of Gold) possesses the power to truly "open" metals for alchemical transformation, dismissing common substances like salt, vitriol, fire, or antimony as insufficient.</summary>
<keywords>Gold (Sol), Red Lion, Opening of Metals, Golden Chain, Antimony, Sal Ammoniac, Alchemical Spirits</keywords>

--- Page 255 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the "opening" of metals mentioned on the previous page. The text transitions from criticizing insufficient materials like common salt and antimony to explaining the specific role of the "spirit of the world" found within Gold (Sol).</meta>

### ...or the Salt of the Philosophers.

to advance <note>This continues the sentence from the previous page: "...to advance [the work]."</note>, one would still find nothing in there: for all these Salts are brought forth from the **spirit of the world**, which abundantly rests in the <term>Spirit</term> <note>Symbol: ☊; likely referring to the *Anima* or the vital soul of the matter.</note> of this <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>: for that is why <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note> says that he must help perform all things.

I believe that no person will be found who will properly know the powers of <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>, along with those of <term>Fire</term> <note>Symbol: △</note>, and those of <term>Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. Yet there are two Mercuries, of which one is Metallic, and the other is a Mineral: and so it is also among the Sulphurs, that the one is of a Metallic Nature, and the other is inclined toward the Salts. 

He who now knows how to make this distinction will very well obtain the Friend of <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>, to move him to become a "bath" <note>An alchemical "bath" refers to a solvent or medium in which substances are dissolved or cleansed.</note> for the Metals and Minerals: for these are very devoted to the <term>Spirit</term> <note>Symbol: ☊</note> of the <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>. I myself have not long known that one could obtain a sweet spirit from <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note> through a simple boiling <note>original: "slecht koken." In this context, 'slecht' means simple or straightforward rather than 'bad'.</note>. 

Concerning this, I intend to treat hereafter in another Treatise, which I shall call the **Green Lion** <note>The "Green Lion" is a famous alchemical symbol often representing vitriol or the raw, acidic energy of nature used to dissolve gold.</note>. May the favorable Reader please be satisfied with this for now, until I shall be able to share somewhat more with them, whereby the **Art** <note>The "Art" (Konst) is the standard term for Alchemy.</note> may be honored and exalted, which now is so lamentably trampled underfoot unjustly by many.

->T H E   E N D.<-

<summary>This concluding page explains that the true power to open metals lies in the "spirit of the world" found within Gold (Sol). The author distinguishes between metallic and mineral versions of Mercury and Sulphur, ending with a promise of a future work titled "The Green Lion."</summary>
<keywords>Spirit of the World, Gold, Mercury, Sulphur, Green Lion, Alchemical Bath, Metallic Nature, The Art</keywords>
